16.09808pt\raise-26.14581pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.1743pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 33.1743pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C_{4}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\right]:=[[C_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}C_{2}]\to[C_{3}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}C_{4}]],

where the diagram in the brackets is a commutative diagram in the same
\infty
-category.
For an operator
F
acting on
C
, we will use the brackets
[C]^{F}
to denote the derived eigenspaces and, if
C
is a concrete complex and
F
an operator acting on
C
, the brackets
(C)^{F}
or simply
C^{F}
, to denote the non-derived ones.

Our cohomology groups will be equipped with a canonical topology. To talk about it in a systematic way, we will work rationally in the category of locally convex
K
-vector spaces and integrally in the category of pro-discrete
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
-modules. For details the reader may consult
[
17
, Sec. 2.1, 2.2]
. To summarize quickly:

(1)

C_{K}
is the category of convex
K
-vector spaces;
it is a quasi-abelian category.
We will denote the left-bounded derived
\infty
-category of
C_{K}
by

{\mathscr{D}}(C_{K})
.
A morphism of complexes that is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{K})
, i.e., its cone is strictly exact, will be called a
strict quasi-isomorphism
.
The associated cohomology objects are denoted by
8
8

8

LH
stands for “left heart”.

\widetilde{H}^{n}(E)\in{LH}(C_{K})
; they are called
classical
if the natural map
\widetilde{H}^{n}(E)\to{H}^{n}(E)
is an isomorphism
9
9

9

In our situations this is usually equivalent to
H^{n}(E)
being separated.
.

(2)

We will often work in a slightly more general setting. Let
A_{K}:={\rm LH}(C_{K})
. It is an abelian category and we have
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{K})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathscr{D}}(A_{K})
. Let
B\in C_{K}
be a topological algebra over
K
. We will denote by
A_{B}
the abelian subcategory of
A_{K}
of
B
-modules. We set
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{B}):={\mathscr{D}}(A_{B})
.

(3)

For the default tensor product (over
K
) in
C_{K}
we have chosen the projective tensor product (which commutes with projective limits). It is left exact.

(4)

Objects in the category
PD_{K}
of pro-discrete
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
-modules are topological
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
-modules that are countable inverse limits, as topological
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
-modules, of discrete
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
-modules
M^{i}
,
i\in{\bf N}
.
It is a quasi-abelian category.
Inside
PD_{K}
we distinguish the category
PC_{K}
of pseudocompact
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
-modules, i.e., pro-discrete modules
M\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{i}M_{i}
such that each
M_{i}
is of finite length (we note that if
K
is a finite extension of
{\bf Q}_{p}
this is equivalent to
M
being profinite).
It is an abelian category.

(5)

There is a tensor product functor from the category of pro-discrete
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
-modules to convex
K
-vector spaces:

(-){\otimes}K:PD_{K}\to C_{K},\quad M\mapsto M\otimes_{{\mathscr{O}}_{K}}K.

Since
C_{K}
admits filtered inductive limits, the functor
(-){\otimes}K
extends
to a functor
(-){\otimes}K:{\rm Ind}(PD_{K})\to C_{K}
. The functor
(-){\otimes}K
is right exact but not, in general, left exact.
We will consider its (compatible) left derived functors

(-){\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}K:{\mathscr{D}}^{-}(PD_{K})\to{\rm Pro}({\mathscr{D}}^{-}(C_{K})),\quad(-){\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}K:{\mathscr{D}}^{-}({\rm Ind}(PD_{K}))\to{\rm Pro}({\mathscr{D}}^{-}(C_{K})).

If
E
is a complex of torsion free and
p
-complete (i.e.,
E\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{n}E/p^{n}
) modules from
PD_{K}
then the natural map

E{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}K\to E{\otimes}K

is a strict quasi-isomorphism
[
17
, Prop. 2.6]
.

Finally, we will use freely the notation and results from
[
20
]
.

2.
Hyodo-Kato rigidity revisited

The original Hyodo-Kato morphism
[
34
]
works for semistable (formal) schemes.
It can not be transferred to rigid analytic varieties because, a priori, it is dependent on the choice of the uniformizer of the base field (which varies for local semistable models).
A more careful data keeping allowed Beilinson
[
3
]
to make it independent of choices in the case of proper schemes.
We adapt here his technique to semistable formal schemes and add some extra functoriality by
lifting the morphism to the derived
\infty
-category.

This gives us local
Hyodo-Kato morphisms for rigid analytic varieties; the extra functoriality will be crucial
for the globalization of these maps for
rigid analytic and dagger varieties discussed in Chapter
4
(it makes it possible to glue
local maps from an hypercover by semistable formal schemes).

2.1.
Preliminaries

We gather in this section basic properties of period rings, isogenies, and
\varphi
-modules that we will need in the paper.

2.1.1.
Period rings

We will review first the definitions of the rings of periods that we will need. We follow here Beilinson
[
3
, 1.14, 1.19]
, where the reader can find more details. Beilinson’s definitions are a slight modification of the classical ones; they stress the dependence on choices in a better way.

(i)
Arithmetic setting.
Let
S=S_{K}:=\operatorname{Spf}{\mathscr{O}}_{K}^{\times}
,
S^{0}:=\operatorname{Spf}{\mathscr{O}}_{F}^{0}
. We denote the corresponding log-structures by
{\mathscr{L}}={\mathscr{L}}_{K}
and
{\mathscr{L}}^{0}_{K}
, respectively. Note that the second log-structure can be conveniently described by the pre-log structure

{\mathscr{O}}_{K}\setminus\{0\}\to{\mathscr{O}}_{F},a\mapsto[\overline{a}]
, where
\overline{a}:=a\mod{\mathfrak{m}}_{K}
and
[-]
denotes the Teichmüller lift.

Consider the algebra
{\mathscr{O}}_{F}[T]

with the log-structure associated to
T
.
We denote by
r_{F}^{\rm PD}
the associated
p
-adic divided powers polynomial algebra.
In a more natural in
K
way, we can write

r_{F}^{\rm PD}
as
r_{K}^{\rm PD,0}
– the
p
-adic completion of
{\mathscr{O}}_{F}<t_{a}>
, the divided powers polynomial algebra generated by
t_{a},a\in({\mathfrak{m}}_{K}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{K})\setminus\{0\}
, with
t_{a^{\prime}}=[a^{\prime}/a]t_{a}
.
We denote by
r_{K}^{\rm PD}
the
p
-adic completion of the subalgebra of the PD algebra
{\mathscr{O}}_{F}<t_{a}>
generated by
t_{a}
and
t^{ne}_{a}/n!,n\geq 1
.
The log-structure is induced by the
t_{a}
’s, Frobenius action by
t_{a}\mapsto t_{a}^{p}
, and monodromy by the derivation sending
t_{a}\mapsto t_{a}
.
Set
E=E_{K}:=\operatorname{Spf}r^{\rm PD}_{K}
,
E^{0}=E^{0}_{K}:=\operatorname{Spf}r^{\rm PD,0}_{K}
.
We have canonical exact embeddings
i_{0}:S^{0}\hookrightarrow E,i^{*}_{0}(t_{a})=[a]\in{\mathscr{L}}^{0}_{K}
,

i^{0}_{0}:S^{0}\hookrightarrow E^{0},i^{0,*}_{0}(t_{a})=[a]\in{\mathscr{L}}^{0}_{K}
.

We have an exact closed embedding
S^{0}_{1}\hookrightarrow S_{1}
.
Retractions
\pi_{l}

are given by maps
\pi^{*}_{l}:{\mathscr{L}}^{0}_{1}\to{\mathscr{L}}_{1},a\mapsto l_{a},
with
l_{a^{\prime}}=[a^{\prime}/a]l_{a}
.
Every retraction
\pi_{l}:S_{1}\to S^{0}_{1}
yields a
k^{0}
-structure on
S_{1}
, hence an
exact closed embedding
i_{l}:S_{1}\hookrightarrow E_{1}
,
i^{*}_{l}(t_{a})=l_{a}
.

(ii)
Geometric setting.
Let
\overline{S}:=\operatorname{Spf}{\mathscr{O}}^{\times}_{C}
. We denote its log-structure by
\overline{{\mathscr{L}}}
. We normalize the valuation on
C
by
v(p)=1
.
Let
\overline{{\mathscr{L}}}^{0}
be the log-structure on
\overline{S}^{0}:=\operatorname{Spf}W(\overline{k})
generated by the pre-log structure
{\mathscr{O}}_{C}\setminus\{0\}\to W(\overline{k}),a\mapsto[\overline{a}]
,

\overline{a}:=a\mod{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{K}}
.
Then
\overline{{\mathscr{L}}}^{0}
has a natural Frobenius action compatible with the Frobenius:
\varphi([a])=[a^{p}]
.
There is an exact embedding
\overline{S}^{0}_{1}\hookrightarrow\overline{S}_{1}
.

We will denote by
{\bf A}^{\times}_{\operatorname{cr}}
the period ring
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}
equipped with the unique log-structure
{\mathscr{L}}_{\operatorname{cr}}
extending the one on
{\mathscr{O}}^{\times}_{C,1}
. Let
J_{\operatorname{cr}}
be the PD-ideal,
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}/J_{\operatorname{cr}}\simeq{\mathscr{O}}_{C,1}
.
Set
{\mathscr{E}}_{\operatorname{cr}}:=\operatorname{Spf}{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{\times}
.
The exact embedding
\operatorname{Spec}{\mathscr{O}}_{C,1}^{\times}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{\operatorname{cr},n}
given by the Fontaine map
\theta:{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}\to{\mathscr{O}}_{C}
is
a PD-thickening in the crystalline site of

{\mathscr{O}}_{{F},1}
.

Recall the definition of the period ring
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
.
Let
\log:{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{*}/\overline{k}^{*}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
be the logarithm: the unique homomorphism which extends the logarithm on
(1+J_{\operatorname{cr}})^{*}
,
where
J_{\operatorname{cr}}=(p,{\rm Ker}\,\theta)
.
Then
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
is defined as the universal
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-algebra equipped with a homomorphism of monoids
\log:{\mathscr{L}}_{\operatorname{cr}}/\overline{k}^{*}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
extending the above
\log
on
{\bf A}^{*}_{\operatorname{cr}}
.
Since
v:{\mathscr{L}}_{\operatorname{cr}}/{\bf A}^{*}_{\operatorname{cr}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\bf Q}_{\geq 0}
, it is clear that, for any
\lambda\in{\mathscr{L}}_{\operatorname{cr}}/\overline{k}^{*}
with
v(\lambda)\neq 0
, the element
\log(\lambda)
freely generates
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
over
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
, i.e.,
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}[\log(\lambda)]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
.
The Frobenius action extends to
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
via universality.
The monodromy
N
is the
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-derivation on
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
such that
N(\log(\lambda))=-v(\lambda)
.
We have
N\varphi=p\varphi N
.
Moreover, any
\lambda
as above yields a retraction

s^{*}_{\lambda}:{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}},s^{*}_{\lambda}(\log(\lambda))=0
.
If
\lambda\in{\mathscr{L}}_{\varphi}:=\{\lambda\in{\mathscr{L}}_{\operatorname{cr}}:\varphi(\lambda)=\lambda^{p}\}
then
s^{*}_{\lambda}
is compatible with Frobenius action.

Now, recall the definition of the period ring
\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{l,\operatorname{st}}
.
Let
r\in{\bf Q}_{>0}
. Denote by
{\mathbb{A}}^{(r)}_{W(\overline{k})}
, the log affine space, i.e., the formal scheme
\operatorname{Spf}W(\overline{k})\{t_{a}\},a\in\tau_{r}
, with
t_{a^{\prime}}=[a^{\prime}/a]t_{a}
.
Here
\tau_{r}:=\{a\in\overline{{\mathscr{L}}}_{1}^{0}:v(a)=r\}
.
The log-structure is generated by the
t_{a}
’s.
The map
i_{r}:\overline{S}^{0}_{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{(r)}_{W(\overline{k})}
,
i_{r}^{*}(t_{a})=a,
can be extended to a
map
i_{l}:\overline{S}_{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{(r)}_{W(\overline{k})}
by choosing
l_{a}:=i_{l}^{*}(t_{a})\in\overline{{\mathscr{L}}}_{1}
that lifts
a
.

We have the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}^{(r)}_{W(\overline{k})}\times_{W(\overline{k})}{\mathscr{E}}_{\operatorname{cr},n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{S}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(i_{l},\theta)}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{\operatorname{cr},n}}

Let
i_{l,\operatorname{st}}:\overline{S}_{1}\hookrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}
be the PD-envelope of
(i_{l},\theta)
over
{\mathscr{E}}_{\operatorname{cr},n}
. We write
{\mathscr{E}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}=\operatorname{Spec}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}
and set

\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st}}:=\operatorname{lim}_{n}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n},\quad\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{l,\operatorname{st}}:=\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st}}[\tfrac{1}{p}],\quad{\mathscr{E}}_{l,st}:=\operatorname{Spf}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st}}.

We note that
\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{l,\operatorname{st}}
is a Banach space over
F
(which makes it easier to handle topologically than
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
). Frobenius action is given by
t_{a}\mapsto t_{a}^{p}
and the monodromy
operator by
N_{l}:=t_{a}\partial_{t_{a}}
. We have the exact sequence

(2.1)

0\to{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}\to\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle N_{l}}}{{\to}}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}\to 0.

Every lifting of
l
to
\lambda\in{\mathscr{L}}_{\varphi}
yields a map
s^{*}_{\lambda}:\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}\to{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n},s^{*}_{\lambda}(t_{a}):=\lambda_{a}
, which is compatible with Frobenius action, and an identification
\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}<t_{a}\lambda^{-1}_{a}-1>
.

Let
\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st}}^{N_{l}{\text{-nilp}}}

be the
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-subalgebra of
\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st}}

formed by the elements killed by a power of
t_{a}\partial_{t_{a}}
. It is the divided powers polynomial algebra
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}<\log(t_{a}\lambda_{a}^{-1})>
. There is a
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-linear isomorphism

\kappa_{l}:{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st},{\bf Q}_{p}}^{N_{l}{\text{-nilp}}}

which sends a generator
\log(\lambda)
of
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
, where
\lambda
lifts
l
, to
-\log(t_{a}\lambda^{-1}_{a})\in\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st}}^{N_{l}{\text{-nilp}}}
.
It is compatible with the action of
{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
, Frobenius, and it identifies
N
on
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
with the action of
rt_{a}\partial_{t_{a}}
.

Finally we have maps to
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
. We will normalize them for the rest of the paper at
p
.
That is, we fix a lift
[\tilde{p}]\in{\mathscr{L}}_{\varphi}
of
p
and define the
maps:

\iota=\iota_{p}:\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{p,\operatorname{st}}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},\quad\iota=\iota_{p}:=\iota_{p}\kappa_{p}:{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}.

The first map is obtained by sending
t_{p}
to
p
; the second map, by sending
\log([\tilde{p}])
to
-\log(p/[\tilde{p}])
. Otherwise saying, we can set

\displaystyle\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:=\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{p,\operatorname{st}}:={\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}<t_{p}[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}-1>^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}[\tfrac{1}{p}],\quad{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:={\bf B}^{+}_{p,\operatorname{st}}:={\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}[\log([\widetilde{p}])],

\displaystyle\kappa:{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}},\,\log([\widetilde{p}])\mapsto-\log(t_{p}[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}),\quad\iota:{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},\,\log([\widetilde{p}])\mapsto-\log(p[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}),

\displaystyle\iota:\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},\,t_{p}[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}\mapsto p[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}.

2.1.2.
Tensoring with period rings

(1) Let
M
be a bounded complex of Banach spaces, which are topological
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-modules. We define the topological tensor product
M\otimes_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}C
as the algebraic tensor product equipped with the quotient topology induced from
M
via the map
\theta
.
This product tends to be compatible with strict quasi-isomorphisms:

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
M,M^{\prime}
be bounded complexes of Banach spaces, which are flat
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-modules. Let
\alpha:M\to M^{\prime}
be a strict quasi-isomorphism. Then the induced morphism

\alpha\otimes\operatorname{Id}:M\otimes_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}C\to M^{\prime}\otimes_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}C

is a strict quasi-isomorphism as well.

Proof.

Let
C(\alpha)
denote the mapping fiber of
\alpha
. It is a bounded complex of Banach spaces. We claim that the complex

C(\alpha\otimes\operatorname{Id})=[M\otimes_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}C\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\alpha\otimes\operatorname{Id}}}\,M^{\prime}\otimes_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}C]\simeq C(\alpha)\otimes_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}C

is strictly acyclic. Indeed, since
M,M^{\prime}
are bounded and built from flat
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-modules,
this is so algebraically.
Now the terms of
C(\alpha\otimes\operatorname{Id})
are Banach spaces as quotients of Banach spaces by closed subspaces
and the Open Mapping Theorem implies that a complex of Banach spaces is strictly acyclic if and only if
it is acyclic (apply the OMT to the isomorphism

{\rm Im}(d_{i})\to{\rm Ker}(d_{i+1})
which are both Banach spaces since
d_{i}

and
d_{i+1}
are continuous).
∎

(2) Similarly, for a bounded complex
M
of Banach spaces, which are topological
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-modules, we define the topological tensor product
M\otimes_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{i})
,
i\geq 0
, as the algebraic tensor product equipped with the quotient topology induced from
M
. We have analog of the Lemma
2.2
in this setting.

We will denote this tensor product by

M\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{i}),\quad i\geq 0.

(3) For a bounded complex
M
of Banach spaces, which are topological
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-modules,
we define

M\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}:={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{i}(M\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{i})).

We have analog of the Lemma
2.2
in this setting as well.

2.1.3.
Isogenies

We recall now some terminology from
[
6
, Sec. 1.1]
(see also
[
1
, Sec. 2.3]
).

Let
{\mathscr{C}}
be an additive category (or
\infty
-category). A map
f:P\to Q
is an
isogeny
if there exists
g:Q\to P
and an integer
N>0
such that
gf=N\operatorname{Id}_{P}
and
fg=N\operatorname{Id}_{Q}
(in the homotopy category.)
An object
X\in{\mathscr{C}}
is
bounded torsion
if it is killed by some
N
, i.e., if
N\operatorname{Id}_{X}=0
(also in the homotopy category). If
{\mathscr{C}}
is an additive category, we denote by
{\mathscr{C}}\otimes{\bf Q}
the category
with the same objects as
{\mathscr{C}}
, with a functor
{\mathscr{C}}\to{\mathscr{C}}\otimes{\bf Q},X\mapsto X_{{\bf Q}}
, and
with
{\rm{Hom}}(X_{{\bf Q}},Y_{{\bf Q}})={\rm{Hom}}(X,Y)\otimes{\bf Q}
.
Then
{\mathscr{C}}\otimes{\bf Q}
is the localization of
{\mathscr{C}}
with respect to isogenies;
for
X\in{\mathscr{C}}
, we have
X_{{\bf Q}}=0
, i.e.,
X
is isogenous to
0
,
if and only if
X
is a bounded torsion object. If
{\mathscr{C}}
is abelian then
{\mathscr{C}}\otimes{\bf Q}
is abelian as well and it equal to the quotient
{\mathscr{C}}_{{\bf Q}}
of
{\mathscr{C}}
modulo the Serre subcategory of bounded torsion objects.

Let
{\mathscr{C}}
be a stable
\infty
-category equipped with a
t
-structure. If a map is an isogeny then it induces isogenies on all cohomology groups
H^{n}
,
n\in{\bf Z}
, in the heart
{\mathscr{C}}^{\heartsuit}
. For maps between bounded object the opposite is true as well: the map
f:P\to Q
of bounded objects is an isogeny, if, for each
n
, the map
H^{n}P\to H^{n}Q
is an isogeny.
In particular,
X\in{\mathscr{C}}
is isogeneous to
0
if each
H^{n}(X)
is a bounded torsion group.

Remark 2.3
.

Consider the tensor product functor in the top row of the diagram:

\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}({\rm PD}_{K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}(C_{K})}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{D}}({\rm PD}_{K})_{{\bf Q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)_{K}}

It factors naturally through the isogeny category; we will denote so obtained functor from
{\mathscr{D}}({\rm PD}_{K})_{{\bf Q}}
to
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{K})
by
(-)_{K}
.

2.1.4.
\varphi
-modules

A Frobenius on an
{\mathscr{O}}_{F}
-module is a
\varphi_{F}
-linear endomorphism. Let
R
be an
{\mathscr{O}}_{F}
-algebra equipped with a Frobenius
\varphi_{R}
. For an
R
-module
M
, a Frobenius on
M
compatible with the
R
-module structure is an
R
-linear map
\varphi_{M}:\varphi_{R}^{*}M\to M
. Pairs
(M,\varphi_{M})
form an abelian tensor
{\bf Z}_{p}
-category
R_{\varphi}
-
{\rm Mod}
. Let
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(R)
be its bounded derived
\infty
-category.

Remark 2.4
.

By
[
37
, Rem. 7.1.1.16]
, we may (and often will) identify the
\infty
-category
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(R)
with the
\infty
-category of left modules from
{\mathscr{D}}(R)
over the
E_{1}
-ring
R\{\varphi\}
defined as the abelian group

R[\varphi]
with multiplication rules
\varphi a=\varphi(a)\varphi,
for
a\in R
. We will do the same for other categories of left modules over associative rings that appear in this paper. This sometimes may entail unbounding the derived category to the left but this will not cause problems.

Consider the bounded derived
\infty
-categories
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(R),{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(R)_{{\bf Q}}
of bounded complexes of
\varphi
-modules over
R
. Then
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(R)_{{\bf Q}}
is the quotient of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(R)
modulo the full subcategory of complexes with bounded torsion cohomology.

We need to discuss projective resolutions. For an
R
-module
M
, set
M_{\varphi}:=\oplus_{n\geq 0}\varphi^{n*}_{R}M
and equip it with the evident Frobenius. The functor
R
-
{\rm Mod}\to R_{\varphi}
-
{\rm Mod}
,
M\mapsto M_{\varphi}
, is left adjoint to the forgetful functor
R_{\varphi}
-
{\rm Mod}\to R
-
{\rm Mod},

(M,\varphi_{M})\mapsto M
. If follows that, for a projective
R
-module
M
, the
\varphi
-module
M_{\varphi}
is a projective object of

R_{\varphi}
-
{\rm Mod}
.

For every
M=(M,\varphi_{M})\in R_{\varphi}
-
{\rm Mod}
, there is a natural short exact sequence

0\to(\varphi^{*}_{R}M)_{\varphi}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\delta}}\,M_{\varphi}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varepsilon}}\,M\to 0

in
R_{\varphi}
-
{\rm Mod}
. The maps
\varepsilon
and
\delta
are induced, respectively, by adjunction from
\operatorname{Id}_{M}
and the map
\varphi^{*}_{R}M\to M_{\varphi}
that sends
r\otimes m
to
\varphi_{M}(r\otimes m)-r\otimes m\in M\oplus\varphi^{*}_{R}M\subset M_{\varphi}
. Set
\widetilde{M}:=\operatorname{Cone}(\delta)
, so we have a resolution
\varepsilon:\widetilde{M}\to M
. If
M
is a projective
R
-module, this is a projective resolution in
R_{\varphi}
-
{\rm Mod}
.

We will need a version of the above constructions for derived
p
-complete modules. Recall
[
11
, 4.1]
that, for a ring
R
over
{\bf Z}_{p}
,
M\in{\mathscr{D}}(R)
is called
p
-completely flat if
M\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}R/p\in{\mathscr{D}}(R/p)
is concentrated in degree
0
, where it is a flat
R/p
-module. If
R
has
bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion and an
R
-module
M
is derived
p
-complete and
p
-completely flat then
M
is a classically
p
-complete

R
-module concentrated in degree
0
, with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion, such that
M/p^{n}M
is flat over

R/p^{n}R
, for all
n\geq 1
(see
[
11
, Lemma 4.7]
). And, conversely, if
M
is a classically
p
-complete

R
-module concentrated in degree
0
, with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion, such that
M/p^{n}M
is flat over

R/p^{n}R
, for all
n\geq 1
, then
M
is
p
-completely flat.

Now we specialize to
R=W(k)
. Then, a
W(k)
-module
M
is derived
p
-complete and
p
-completely flat if and only if
M
is a classically
p
-complete

W(k)
-module concentrated in degree
0
, such that
M/p^{n}M
is flat over
W_{n}(k)
, for all
n\geq 1
(equivalently
M
is
p
-torsion-free and classically
p
-complete over

W(k)
). We note that such a module
M
can always be written as the
p
-adic completion of a free
W(k)
-module
M_{0}
hence it is a projective object in the category of derived
p
-complete modules
10
10

10

We use here that, if a
W(k)
-module
N
is derived
p
-complete then
{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)}(M,N)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)}(M_{0},N)
.
.

It follows that the above algebraic construction goes through once we derived
p
-adically complete the objects. That is, if we denote by
\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}(W(k))
the full
\infty
-subcategory of
{\mathscr{D}}(W(k))
spanned by complexes built from derived
p
-complete modules
11
11

11

By
[
45
, Tag 09IU]
, this is the same as the full subcategory of
{\mathscr{D}}(W(k))
od derived
p
-complete complexes.
and, similarly, if we denote by
\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}_{\varphi}(W(k))
the full
\infty
-subcategory of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(W(k))
spanned by complexes built from derived
p
-complete
\varphi
-modules
then, for a
\varphi
-modules
M=M[0]\in\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}_{\varphi}(W(k))
, which is
p
-completely flat, the
p
-adic completion
\widehat{M}_{\varphi}\in\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}_{\varphi}(W(k))
of
M_{\varphi}
is a projective derived
p
-complete
\varphi
-module and we have a projective resolution of derived
p
-complete
\varphi
-modules

(2.5)

0\to\widehat{(\varphi^{*}_{R}{M})_{\varphi}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\delta}}\,\widehat{M_{\varphi}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varepsilon}}\,M\to 0.

We set
\widetilde{M}:={\rm Cone}(\delta)
. We note that, for a complex
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
of
p
-completely flat, derived
p
-complete
\varphi
-modules over
W(k)
, the functor
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mapsto\widetilde{M}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
preserves quasi-isomorphisms (since so does the functor
M^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mapsto\widehat{M_{\varphi}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
because

\widehat{M_{\varphi}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
is equal to
\widehat{M_{\varphi}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
).

We will often use the fact that the canonical functor
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))\to\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}_{\varphi}(W(k))
, where
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))
is the full subcategory of
\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}_{\varphi}(W(k))
spanned by complexes built from
p
-torsion free modules is an equivalence. Indeed,
if
M
is a derived
p
-complete
W(k)
-module then we can find an exact sequence (see
[
45
, Tag 09AT]
)

0\to M_{1}\to M_{0}\to M\to 0,

where
M_{0},M_{1}
are derived
p
-complete and torsion free. Moreover, if
M
is a
\varphi
-module, we can lift its Frobenius to this exact sequence. This and the existence of the projective resolutions (
2.5
) yield the desired equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

Let
R:=r_{K}^{\rm PD}
.
Let
P=(P,\varphi_{P})\in\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}_{\varphi}(R),

Q=(Q,\varphi_{Q})\in{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))
. We assume that the complex
P
is built from
p
-completely flat modules. Denote by
\overline{P}
the cofiber of
P\to P\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}W(k)
viewed as an object of
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))
.

Lemma 2.6
.

If the Frobenius on
Q_{\bf Q}
is invertible
12
12

12

This means that Frobenius map
\varphi_{Q_{{\bf Q}}}:\varphi^{*}_{W(k)}Q_{{\bf Q}}\to Q_{{\bf Q}}
is a quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(W(k))_{{\bf Q}}
.

then

{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}(Q,\overline{P})_{\bf Q}={\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}(Q_{\bf Q},\overline{P}_{\bf Q})=0,

where the
{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}
is taken in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))_{\bf Q}
.

Proof.

We claim that we have the short exact sequence of complexes of
\varphi
-modules over
W(k)
(
p
-torsion-free and
p
-complete)

(2.7)

0\to IP\to P\to P\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}W(k)\to 0,

where
I\subset R
is the kernel of the projection
R\to W(k)
and we set

IP:=I\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm L}_{R}P\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}I\widehat{\otimes}_{R}P
. Indeed, because
P_{n}
is a complex of flat
R_{n}
-modules we have a compatible family of exact sequences

0\to P_{n}\otimes_{R}I_{n}\to P\otimes_{R}R_{n}\to P_{n}\otimes_{R}W_{n}(k)\to 0

Passing to the limit we get the short exact sequence

0\to\operatorname{lim}_{n}(P_{n}\otimes_{R}I_{n})\to\operatorname{lim}_{n}(P\otimes_{R}R_{n})\to\operatorname{lim}_{n}(P_{n}\otimes_{R}W_{n}(k))\to 0

Since
P
is derived
p
-complete and its terms are
p
-completely flat modules, the natural morphism
P\to\operatorname{lim}_{n}(P\otimes_{R}R_{n})
is a quasi-isomorphism (in fact, an isomorphism)
[
45
, Tag 091Z]
and the above exact sequence yields the exact sequence (
2.7
).

From the exact sequence (
2.7
) we get a distinguished triangle

{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}(Q,IP)\to{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}(Q,P)\to{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}(Q,P\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}W(k)),

where

{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}
is computed in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))
. To prove the lemma, it suffices to show that

{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}(Q,IP)_{\bf Q}=0
.

Assume, for a moment, that
Q
is concentrated in degree
0
.
For any derived
p
-complete
\varphi
-module
M
over
W(k)
, we can compute
{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}(Q,M)
using the
projective resolution
\widetilde{Q}
of
Q
from (
2.5
).
We get a two-term complex
C(Q,M)
with

\displaystyle C^{0}(Q,M)

\displaystyle={\rm{Hom}}(\widehat{Q_{\varphi}},M)\simeq{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)_{\varphi}}({Q_{\varphi}},M)\simeq{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)}(Q,M),

\displaystyle C^{1}(Q,M)

\displaystyle={\rm{Hom}}(\widehat{\varphi^{*}(Q)_{\varphi}},M)\simeq{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)_{\varphi}}({\varphi^{*}(Q)_{\varphi}},M)\simeq{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)}(\varphi^{*}(Q),M),

and the differential
d=d_{1}-d_{2}:C^{0}(Q,M)\to C^{1}(Q,M)
, where
d_{1}(A)=A\varphi_{Q}
,
d_{2}(A)=\varphi_{M}\varphi^{*}(A)
.
Let
C_{*}(Q,M)
be the complex with the same terms as
C(Q,M)
but the differential simply
d_{1}
.
Since we assumed that the Frobenius action on
Q_{{\bf Q}}
is invertible, the complex
C_{*}(Q,M)_{\bf Q}
is acyclic.

We go back now to general
Q
. To show that
{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}(Q,IP)_{\bf Q}=0
we we may assume that
P
is concentrated in degree
0
. Indeed, since
P
is bounded, we can detach one term of the complex after another using the fact that
IP=I\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm L}_{R}P
. We will denote by
C(Q,M)
, for
M
as above, the total complex of the double complex obtained by applying
C(-,M)
to all the terms of
Q
. We will prove that
C(Q,IP)_{\bf Q}
is acyclic by defining a finite filtration on
IP
, by derived
p
-complete
W(k)
-submodules, such that
C(Q,\operatorname{gr}^{j}IP)\simeq C_{*}(Q,\operatorname{gr}^{j}IP)
. (Note that then the gradings
\operatorname{gr}^{i}IP
are also derived
p
-complete and, since
Q
is built from projective modules, the functor
C(Q,-)
is exact.) The latter complex is acyclic by the argument presented above.

Let
I^{[j]}
,
j\geq 1
, be the ideal of
R
formed by series
\sum a_{i}t^{[i]}
with
a_{0}=\ldots=a_{j-1}=0
. We have
I=I^{[1]}
. We set

I^{[j]}P:=I^{[j]}\widehat{\otimes}^{\rm L}_{R}P\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}I^{[j]}\widehat{\otimes}_{R}P.

Since
R/I^{[j]}\simeq W(k)^{\oplus j-1}
,
I^{[j]}P\hookrightarrow P
(argue as in the proof of (
2.7
) above), and it is a derived
p
-complete module.
Since
\varphi(I^{[j]})\subset I^{[pj]}
, one has
C(Q,I^{[j]}P/I^{[j+1]}P)=C_{*}(Q,I^{[j]}P/I^{[j+1]}P)
. It remains to show that
C(Q,I^{[n]}P)
is quasi-isomorphic to
C_{*}(Q,I^{[n]}P)
for
n
sufficiently large (then the soughed-after finite filtration is
I^{[j]}P
,
j\leq n.
)

By assumption, for
m
sufficiently large, there is
\psi:Q\to\varphi^{*}Q
such that
\varphi_{Q}\psi=p^{m}\operatorname{Id}_{Q},\psi\varphi_{Q}=p^{m}\operatorname{Id}_{\varphi^{*}(Q)}
. For
n
sufficiently large, we have
\varphi(I^{[n]})\subset p^{m+1}I^{[pn]}
. Hence
d_{2}
on
C(Q,I^{[n]}P)
is divisible by
p^{m+1}
. Set
f:=\psi^{\tau}(p^{-m-1}d_{2})\in\operatorname{End}(C^{0}(Q,I^{[n]}P))
; then
d_{2}=pd_{1}f
, i.e.,
d=d_{1}(1-pf)
. We used here that, since
Q
is built from projective modules and
I^{[n]}P
is derived
p
-complete, we have

\displaystyle C^{0}(Q,I^{[n]}P)={\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)}(Q,I^{[n]}P)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)}(Q,I^{[n]}P),

\displaystyle C^{1}(Q,I^{[n]}P)={\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)}(\varphi^{*}(Q),I^{[n]}P)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)}(\varphi^{*}(Q),I^{[n]}P).

Moreover, since
{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)}(Q,I^{[n]}P)
is derived
p
-complete
[
45
, Tag 0A6E]
, it follows that
(1-pf)
is a quasi-isomorphism (use derived Nakayama Lemma
[
45
, Tag 0G1U]
). This yields the quasi-isomorphism
C(Q,I^{[n]}P)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}C_{*}(Q,I^{[n]}P)
, as wanted.
∎

2.2.
Hyodo-Kato rigidity

Now we pass to the main constructions.

2.2.1.
The Hyodo-Kato section

In this section we will prove the existence of the Hyodo-Kato section in the derived
\infty
-category. We follow faithfully the arguments of Beilinson from
[
3
, Sec. 1.14]
with the following modifications:

(1)

Beilinson works in the setting of proper log-smooth log-schemes hence all of his cohomology complexes are perfect; we replace them with a weaker condition of derived
p
-complete and
p
-completely flat,

(2)

to prove that the Hyodo-Kato section (when linearized) is a quasi-isomorphism Beilinson uses finiteness of Hyodo-Kato cohomology; we replace his argument with the original one due to Hyodo-Kato
[
34
]
.

Since the argument of Beilinson can only be found in a preliminary version of a published paper, for the benefit of the reader and the authors, we supply all the details.

Let
f:X_{1}\to S_{1}
be a log-smooth map with Cartier type reduction, with
X_{1}
fine.
Let
f^{0}:X_{1}^{0}\to S^{0}_{1}
be its pullback to
S^{0}_{1}
.
Let
R:=r_{K}^{\rm PD}
. Recall the definition and basic properties of the arithmetic Hyodo-Kato cohomology and the associated
r^{\rm PD}
cohomology (in the terminology
13
13

13

The notation we use here is a bit different
than the one we used in
[
20
]
.
This is because we have adopted here Beilinson’s approach to the Hyodo-Kato morphism and with it his notation.
The advantage of Beilinson’s notation is that it keeps better track of the underlying data.

from
[
20
, 4.2]
):

(2.8)

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,n}:={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/(S_{1},E_{n})),\quad i_{l}:S_{1}\hookrightarrow E_{1},\quad\mbox{in }{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(R_{n});

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{n}:={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}^{0}/(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{n})),\quad\mbox{in }{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(W_{n}(k));

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}:=\lim_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,n},\quad\mbox{in }\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}_{\varphi}(R);

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1}):=\lim_{n}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{n},\quad\mbox{in }{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k)).

The embedding
i:X^{0}_{1}\hookrightarrow X_{1}
over
i_{l,n}:(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{n})\hookrightarrow(S_{1},E_{n})
yields compatible morphisms
i^{*}_{l,n}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,n}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{n}
,

i^{*}_{l}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})
in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(W_{n}(k))
and
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))
, respectively. These constructions are functorial in
X_{1}
: this is standard (see
[
3
, 1.6]
and use the functorial PD-envelopes from
[
3
, 1.4]
).

Moreover,

(1)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})
is a complex of derived
p
-complete,
p
-completely flat modules over
W(k)
and

(2.9)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{n}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(k)}W_{n}(k),\quad\mbox{in }{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(W_{n}(k));

(2)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}
is a complex of derived
p
-complete,
p
-completely flat modules over
R
and

(2.10)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,n}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}R_{n},\quad\mbox{in }{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(R_{n});

(3)

we have a quasi-isomorphism

(2.11)

i^{*}_{l}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}W(k)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1}),\quad\mbox{in }{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k)).

Now we present the key construction in the Hyodo-Kato theory.

Theorem 2.12
.

(1)

The Frobenius action on
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{\bf Q}
is invertible in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}(W(k))_{{\bf Q}}
.

(2)

The map
i^{*}_{l}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,{\bf Q}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{\bf Q}
admits a unique natural
W(k)
-linear section
\iota_{l}
in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))_{{\bf Q}}
. Its
R
-linear extension is a
quasi-isomorphism in
\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}_{\varphi}(R)_{{\bf Q}}
:

\iota_{l}:(R\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(k)}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1}))_{\bf Q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,{\bf Q}}.

Proof.

Claim (1) is proved in
[
34
, 2.24]
. In fact, Hyodo-Kato prove more: they show that there exists a
p^{d}
-inverse of Frobenius, where
d=\dim X^{0}_{1}
.

For the existence part of claim (2), recall that Beilinson
[
3
, 1.14]
proved it in the case
X_{1}
is proper. We will adapt his argument to our (general) local situation.

Take
P={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}
in Lemma
2.6
. By claim (1) the Frobenius action on
(P\widehat{\otimes}_{R}W(k))_{\bf Q}
is invertible. Moreover,
P
is derived
p
-complete and a complex of completely
p
-adically
flat
R
-modules. Lemma
2.6
implies that the morphism
P_{\bf Q}\to(P\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}W(k))_{\bf Q}
in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))_{{\bf Q}}
admits a unique right inverse
\iota_{l}
, as wanted. We will now show the functoriality of
\iota_{l}
with respect to the maps
f:X_{1}\to S_{1},i_{l}:S_{1}\to E
. Consider a commutative diagram of such maps

\textstyle{X^{\prime}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{X}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{S_{1}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{S}}
\scriptstyle{i_{l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{E^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{E}}
\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{S_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{l}}
\textstyle{E}

It yields the commutative diagram in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X^{\prime}_{1}/R)_{l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{\prime,0}_{1})}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\operatorname{cr}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}_{l}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\operatorname{HK}}^{*}}

and the induced diagram in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k))_{{\bf Q}}

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X^{\prime}_{1}/R)_{l^{\prime},{\bf Q}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{\prime,0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{*}_{l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,{\bf Q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\operatorname{cr}}^{*}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{\operatorname{HK}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{*}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{f_{l^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{f_{l}}

where
f_{l}=\pi_{\operatorname{cr}}^{*}\iota^{*}_{l}
and
f_{l^{\prime}}=\iota^{*}_{l}\pi_{\operatorname{HK}}^{*}
. Hence the left and right corner triangles in the last diagram commute. It suffice thus to show that
f_{l}=f_{l^{\prime}}
.
But we have

\displaystyle i^{*}_{l^{\prime}}f_{l}

\displaystyle=i^{*}_{l^{\prime}}\pi_{\operatorname{cr}}^{*}\iota^{*}_{l}=\pi_{\operatorname{HK}}^{*}i^{*}_{l}\iota^{*}_{l}=\pi_{\operatorname{HK}}^{*},

\displaystyle i^{*}_{l^{\prime}}f_{l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=i^{*}_{l^{\prime}}\iota^{*}_{l^{\prime}}\pi_{\operatorname{HK}}^{*}=\pi_{\operatorname{HK}}^{*}.

Hence, if
\overline{P}
denotes the cofiber of the map
i^{*}_{l^{\prime}}
is suffices to show that
{\mathrm{R}}{\rm{Hom}}_{W(k)_{\varphi}}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1}),\overline{P})_{{\bf Q}}=0
. But this can be done by the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
2.6
.

Consider its
R
-linearization

\iota_{l}:(R\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(k)}P\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}W(k))_{\bf Q}\to P_{\bf Q}.

We need to show that this is a quasi-isomorphism. But this was done by Hyodo-Kato
[
34
, Lemma 4.16, Prop. 4.8]
using the explicit de Rham-Witt presentation of the Hyodo-Kato complex. We are done.
∎

2.2.2.
The Hyodo-Kato morphism

Now, as usual, the Hyodo-Kato morphism can be obtained from the section constructed in Theorem
2.12
. Let
X
be a fine logarithmic formal scheme log-smooth over
S
. Assume that
X_{1}
has Cartier type reduction over
S_{1}
. Let
\varpi
be a uniformizing parameter of
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
.

Corollary 2.13
.

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}({\mathscr{O}}_{K})_{{\bf Q}}

i_{\varpi}:\quad({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(k)}{\mathscr{O}}_{K})_{{\bf Q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)_{{\bf Q}}.

Proof.

Take
E
with
l:=\varpi\mod p\mathfrak{m}_{K}
. This yields an embedding
i_{\pi}:S\hookrightarrow E,i_{\varpi}^{*}(t_{a})=\varpi,a:=\varpi\mod{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}_{K}
.
We start with the quasi-isomorphism from Theorem
2.12

\displaystyle\iota_{l}:({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(k)}R)_{{\bf Q}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,{\bf Q}}.

Tensoring it with
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
(over
R
) we obtain the quasi-isomorphisms

({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(k)}{\mathscr{O}}_{K})_{{\bf Q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}{\mathscr{O}}_{K})_{{\bf Q}}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/{\mathscr{O}}^{\times}_{K})_{{\bf Q}}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)_{{\bf Q}}.

This is the Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphism
i_{\varpi}
we wanted.
∎

2.2.3.
Monodromy action revisited

A
(\varphi,N)
-module over
W(k)
is a triple
(M,\varphi,N)
with
(M,\varphi)
– a
\varphi
-module
over
W(k)
and
N:M\to M
– a
W(k)
-linear endomorphism, called
monodromy
operator, such that

N\varphi=p\varphi N
. The category of
(\varphi,N)
-modules over
W(k)
is abelian. We will denote by

{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(W(k))
the corresponding derived
\infty
-category. Using Remark
2.4
, we will identify this
\infty
-category with the
\infty
-category of left modules from
{\mathscr{D}}(W(k))
over the associative ring
W(k)\{\varphi,N\}
defined as the abelian group
W(k)[\varphi,N]
with multiplication rules

\varphi a=\varphi(a)\varphi,Na=aN,N\varphi=p\varphi N
, for
a\in W(k)
. We denote by
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi,N}(W(k))
the full
\infty
-subcategory of
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(W(k))
spanned by complexes of
p
-torsion-free and
p
-complete modules. We have similar structures over
W_{n}(k)
.

The constructions in (
2.8
) live in respective
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi,N}(-)

\infty
-categories and are functorial in
X_{1}
. The subsequent base changes (
2.9
), (
2.10
), (
2.11
) also lift to the
\infty
-categories
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi,N}(-)
. One way to see this is to use the description of the monodromy action in the paragraphs that follow.

The purpose of this section is to prove the following:

Proposition 2.14
.

The section

\iota_{l}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{\bf Q}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,{\bf Q}}

from Theorem
2.12
commutes with monodromy, i.e., it can be lifted to a section in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi,N}(W(k))_{{\bf Q}}
.

Recall that the monodromy on
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}
is defined as the Gauss-Manin connection and the one on
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})
as its residue at
t=0
. However, to prove Proposition
2.14
we will work with the "integration" of the monodromy action. The argument follows that of Beilinson in
[
3
, Sec. 1.16]
with the modifications mentioned earlier.

(i)
Equivariant structures.
Let
A^{*}
be a cosimplicial algebra. An
A^{*}
-complex
is a complex
M^{*}
of cosimplicial
A^{*}
-modules such that, for every cosimplicial structure map
M^{a}\to M^{b}
, its
A^{b}
-linearization

A^{b}\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{A^{a}}M^{a}\to M^{b}
is a quasi-isomorphism. Denote by
{\mathscr{D}}(A^{*})
the derived
\infty
-category of bounded below
A^{*}
-complexes.
We think of an element of
{\mathscr{D}}(A^{*})
as an
A^{*}
-complex with values in
{\mathscr{D}}(A^{a})
in degree
a
.
For an endomorphism
T
of
A^{*}
,
{\mathscr{D}}_{T}(A^{*})
will denote the derived
\infty
-category of bounded below
A^{*}
-complexes equipped with a
T
-action. We think of an element of
{\mathscr{D}}_{T}(A^{*})
as an
A^{*}
-complex with values in

{\mathscr{D}}_{T^{a}}(A^{a})
in degree
a
(the derived
\infty
-category of
A^{a}\{T^{a}\}
-modules).

Fix an affine scheme
S
as a base. Let
G
be an affine group scheme acting on
X=\operatorname{Spec}A
. Let
[X/G]:=EG\times_{G}X=\operatorname{Spec}A^{*}_{G}
be the simplicial quotient. We have
[X/G]_{m}=X\times G^{m}
. Set
{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(A):={\mathscr{D}}(A^{*}_{G})
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}=\mathfrak{m}_{e}/\mathfrak{m}_{e}^{2}
be the Lie coalgebra of
G
. Let
[X/{\mathfrak{g}}]:=\operatorname{Spec}A^{*}_{\mathfrak{g}}
be the closed subscheme of
[X/G]
defined by the simplicial ideal generated by
{\mathscr{K}}^{2}
, where
{\mathscr{K}}
is the ideal of
[X/G]_{0}\subset[X/G]_{1}
, i.e.,
{\mathscr{K}}=\mathfrak{m}_{e}\otimes A\subset{\mathscr{O}}(G\times X)
. We set
{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathfrak{g}}(A):={\mathscr{D}}(A^{*}_{\mathfrak{g}})
, etc. There is a canonical conservative restriction functor

{\rm Lie}:{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(A)\to{\mathscr{D}}_{\mathfrak{g}}(A).

Moreover:

(1)

Compatible endomorphisms
T_{G}
and
T_{X}
of
G
and
X
yield an endomorphism of
[X/G]
. We have

{\mathscr{D}}_{T,G}(A):={\mathscr{D}}_{T}(A^{*}_{G}),\quad{\mathscr{D}}_{T,\mathfrak{g}}(A):={\mathscr{D}}_{T}(A^{*}_{\mathfrak{g}}).

(2)

For a group scheme
G
, we denote by
G^{\natural}
its PD-completion at the unit
[
3
, Sec. 1.2]
; this is a group PD-scheme, i.e., a scheme equipped with a PD-ideal. For example, we have
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}((U,T))=\Gamma(T,(1+{\mathscr{J}}_{T})^{*})
. If
G
is a group PD-scheme with PD-ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{e}
, then, in the above, we can also consider the Lie coalgebra in PD-sense
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}:=\mathfrak{m}_{e}/\mathfrak{m}_{e}^{[2]}
.

Objects of
{\mathscr{D}}_{T,G}(A)
,
{\mathscr{D}}_{T,\mathfrak{g}}(A)
are called
G
-, resp.
\mathfrak{g}
-equivariant
A
-complexes
. For an
A
-complex
M
, a
G
-equivariant structure
on it is an object
M^{*}_{G}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{G}(A)
together with a quasi-isomorphism
M^{0}_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}M
.

(ii)
Equivariant structures on crystalline cohomology.
Let us go back to the setting of Proposition
2.14
. We note that the objects
(S_{1},E_{n})\in(S_{1}/W_{n}(k))_{\operatorname{cr}}
and
(S^{0}_{1},S_{n}^{0})\in(S^{0}_{1}/W_{n}(k))_{\operatorname{cr}}
have natural
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
-actions:
(S_{1},E_{n})
is a coordinate thickening (with coordinate
t_{a}
),
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
acts on it by homotheties, and we equip
S_{n}^{0}\subset(S_{1},E_{n})
with the induced action. To see the latter action explicitly, we note that, for
(U,T)\in(S^{0}_{1}/W_{n})_{\operatorname{cr}}
, a map
f:(U,T)\to(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{n})
amounts to a lifting
f([a])
of
a\in(\mathfrak{m}_{K}/\mathfrak{m}^{2}_{K})\setminus\{0\}\subset{\mathscr{L}}_{1}^{0}
to
{\mathscr{L}}_{T}^{0}
; these liftings form a
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}((U,T))
-torsor yielding our action.
This
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
-action is compatible with the Frobenius action (
\varphi
acts on
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
as
\varphi^{*}(t):=t^{p}
).

We will now show that the crystalline cohomology complexes
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,n}
,
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{n}
are naturally equipped with
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
-equivariant structures.
Take the simplicial objects
(S_{1},E_{n*})
and
(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{n*})
. Here, for
(U,T)\in(Z/S)_{\operatorname{cr}}
, we wrote
(U,T_{*}):=\check{C}((U,T)/Z)
for the Čech nerve of the crystalline open
(U,T)\in(Z/S)_{\operatorname{cr}}
; it is a simplicial object of
(Z/S)_{\operatorname{cr}}
with terms
(U,T_{a}):=(U,T)^{a+1}
(we use the crystalline site product).
It is easy to see
[
3
, Exercise 1.7]
that
(S_{1},E_{n*})=[(S_{1},E_{n})/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}]
and
(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{n*})=[(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{n})/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}]
. Consider the objects
{\mathrm{R}}f_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{O}}_{X_{1}/W_{n}(k)})
and
{\mathrm{R}}f^{0}_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{O}}_{X^{0}_{1}/W_{n}(k)})
. They are equipped with a Frobenius action. Restricting them to our simplicial objects, we get:

(2.15)

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,n}^{*}:={\mathrm{R}}f_{\operatorname{cr}*}({\mathscr{O}}_{X_{1}/W_{n}(k)})_{(S_{1},E_{n*})}\in\widehat{{\mathscr{D}}}_{\varphi,{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}}(R_{n}),

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{n}^{*}:={\mathrm{R}}f^{0}_{\operatorname{cr}*}({\mathscr{O}}_{X^{0}_{1}/W_{n}(k)})_{(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{n*})}\in{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi,{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}}(W_{n}(k^{\prime})).

Since
({\mathrm{R}}f_{\operatorname{cr}*}({\mathscr{O}}_{X_{1}/W_{n}(k)})_{(S_{1},E_{n*})})^{0}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,n}
and
({\mathrm{R}}f^{0}_{\operatorname{cr}*}({\mathscr{O}}_{X^{0}_{1}/W_{n}(k)})_{(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{n*})})^{0}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{n}
, these are the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
-equivariant structures we wanted.

We are actually interested in
\mathfrak{n}
-action that comes from the above
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
-action, where
\mathfrak{n}
is the Lie algebra of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
in PD-sense (it is a line). The objects from (
2.15
) form projective systems with respect to
n
. Applying
{\mathrm{R}}\lim_{n}
, we get natural
\mathfrak{n}
-structures on
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(Z^{0}_{1})
and
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(Z_{1}/R)_{l}
.
Set
N=e^{-1}t\partial_{t}
,
e=[K^{\prime}:F^{\prime}]
; it is a generator of
\mathfrak{n}\otimes{\bf Q}
. An
\mathfrak{n}_{\bf Q}
-equivariant structure on
W(k^{\prime})_{{\bf Q}}
-complex amounts to an endomorphism
N
. The equality
N\varphi=p\varphi N
comes from the compatibility of the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
-action with Frobenius.

Proof.

(
of Proposition
2.14
)
We proceed as in the proof of Theorem
2.12
but work in the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
-equivariant setting. Namely, we start with the natural map

i^{*}_{l}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,{\bf Q}}^{*}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}}^{*}
, that lifts the map
i^{*}_{l}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,{\bf Q}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}}
, and we look for its
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
-equivariant section (this will be a
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}
-equivariant lift of the section in our proposition). This is supplied by Lemma
2.16
below. The induced map
{\rm Lie}(\iota_{l})
yields a section between the corresponding
\mathfrak{n}_{{\bf Q}}
-equivariant structures. Since it lifts the original section
\iota_{l}
we get the wanted compatibility of the latter with monodromy.
∎

The following lemma was used in the above proof:

Lemma 2.16
.

(1)

The Frobenius action on
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})^{*}_{\bf Q}
is invertible in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}}(W(k^{\prime}))_{{\bf Q}}
.

(2)

The map
i^{*}_{l}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)^{*}_{l,{\bf Q}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})^{*}_{\bf Q}
admits a natural
W(k^{\prime})
-linear section
\iota_{l}
in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi,{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{\natural}}(W(k^{\prime}))_{{\bf Q}}

(2.17)

\iota^{*}_{l}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})^{*}_{\bf Q}{\to}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)^{*}_{l,{\bf Q}}.

Proof.

In claim (1) we need to proof the invertibility, up to a controlled denominator, of the Hyodo-Kato Frobenius. Since, by (
2.15
),

(2.18)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})^{*}_{a}={\mathrm{R}}f^{0}_{\operatorname{cr}*}({\mathscr{O}}_{X^{0}_{1}/W(k)})_{(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{a})}\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X^{0}_{1}/S^{0}_{a}),

where
(S^{0}_{1},S^{0}_{a})
is the crystalline product
(S^{0}_{1},S^{0})^{a+1}
, we can use again
[
34
, 2.24]
. And, recall that, Hyodo-Kato prove more: they show that there exists a
p^{d}
-inverse of Frobenius, where
d=\dim X^{0}_{1}
.

To prove claim (2), take
P={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l}^{*}
and
Q={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})^{*}
. We have

\displaystyle Q_{a}\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X^{0}_{1}/S^{0}_{a}),

\displaystyle P_{a}={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/R)_{l,a}^{*}={\mathrm{R}}f_{\operatorname{cr}*}({\mathscr{O}}_{X_{1}/W(k)})_{(S_{1},E_{a})}\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/E_{a})_{l},

where
(S_{1},E_{a})
is the crystalline product
(S_{1},E)^{a+1}
. More explicitly,

\displaystyle S^{0}_{a}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Spf}W(k)<u_{1}-1,\ldots,u_{a}-1>,

\displaystyle E_{a}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Spf}W(k)<t,u_{1}-1,\ldots,u_{a}-1>;\quad i_{b,0}:S^{0}_{b}\hookrightarrow E_{a},\,t_{s}\mapsto[s],u_{i}\mapsto u_{i},

and the log-structure is induced by
t_{s}
(and its reduction). In particular, the Frobenius on the ideal of the embedding
i_{a,0}
is highly topologically nilpotent. This implies, by an argument identical to the one used in the proof of Theorem
2.12
, that we have a unique section in
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}_{\varphi}(W(k^{\prime}))_{{\bf Q}}

\iota_{l,a}:Q_{a,{\bf Q}}\to P_{a,{\bf Q}}

of the canonical projection

P_{a,{\bf Q}}\to Q_{a,{\bf Q}}

and that this section is functorial with respect to all the cosimplicial maps. Hence it yields the section
\iota^{*}_{l}
from (
2.17
).
Functoriality of this section follows from the functoriality of the individual sections
\iota_{l,a}
.
∎

2.3.
Geometric absolute crystalline cohomology and Hyodo-Kato cohomology

We are now ready to prove the existence of geometric Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphisms.

2.3.1.
The comparison theorem

Let now
\overline{f}:X_{1}\to\overline{S}_{1}
be a map of log-schemes with
X_{1}
integral and quasi-coherent. Assume that
\overline{f}
is the base change of a log-scheme
f:Z_{1}\to S_{1}
, which is log-smooth and with Cartier type reduction. Choose
l
, hence
(S_{1},E)
, as in Section
2.1.1
.
Choose a Frobenius compatible map
\theta_{\lambda}:(\overline{S}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{\operatorname{cr}})\to(S_{1},E)
of PD-thickenings that extends the map
\theta_{1}
, where
\theta
is the canonical map
\theta:\overline{S}\to S
.
This amounts to a choice of
\lambda_{a}:=\theta_{\lambda}(t_{a})\in{\mathscr{L}}_{\varphi}
that lifts
l_{a}\in{\mathscr{L}}_{1}\subset\overline{{\mathscr{L}}}_{1}
.

The following well-known corollary of Theorem
2.12
describes geometric absolute crystalline cohomology
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}):={\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/W(k))
via Hyodo-Kato cohomology (but losing the Galois action).

Corollary 2.19
.

(1)

There is a functorial system of compatible quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}({\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n})

\varepsilon^{R}_{\lambda,n}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(Z_{1}/R)_{l,n}\otimes^{\mathrm{L}}_{R_{n}}{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{n}.

Here the tensor product is taken with respect to the map

\theta^{*}_{\lambda,n}:R_{n}\to{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}
.

(2)

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}({\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}})

\varepsilon^{R}_{\lambda}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(Z_{1}/R)_{l}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{R}{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}).

(3)

There is a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}})

\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\lambda}:({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(Z_{1}^{0})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(k)}{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}})_{{\bf Q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{\bf Q}.

Remark 2.20
.

(1)

The functor
(-)_{\bf Q}:{\mathscr{D}}^{c}({\bf Z}_{p})\to{\mathscr{D}}({C_{{\bf Q}_{p}}})
in (3) is induced from the functor
(-)_{{\bf Q}}
from Remark
2.3
via the map
{\mathscr{D}}^{c}({\bf Z}_{p})\to{\mathscr{D}}({\rm PD}_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
.

(2)

We set
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}):={\rm LMod}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\{\varphi\}}{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}})
.

Proof.

Since
Z_{1}
is log-smooth, claim (1) follows from the log-smooth base change
14
14

14

The proof of which is almost identical to the proof of smooth base change in the case without log-structures, see
[
9
, 2.3.5]
or
[
8
, V.3.5.1]
.
(recall that
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{n}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n})
). Claim (2) follows from claim (1) by taking limits. Claim (3) follows from claim (2) and Theorem
2.12
.
∎

Let
\overline{f}^{0}:X_{1}^{0}\to\overline{S}_{1}^{0}
be the pullback of
\overline{f}
to
\overline{S}^{0}_{1}
. We have the
completed geometric Hyodo-Kato cohomology

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1}):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}^{0}/\overline{S}^{0}).

It is a
W(\overline{k})
-module.
It compares with the arithmetic Hyodo-Kato cohomology via
the log-smooth base change quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(W(\overline{k}))

(2.21)

\beta:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(Z^{0}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(k_{L})}W(\overline{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1}).

Theorem 2.22
.

There is a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}

such that
\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\lambda}=s^{*}_{\lambda}\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}\beta
.

Remark 2.23
.

(1)

Here, for
M={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{1}^{0}),{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})
, we have defined
15
15

15

This is the only context in the paper where we use inductive tensor products.

(2.24)

M_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{r}(M_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{\leq r}_{\operatorname{st}}),\quad M_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{r}(M_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{\leq r}_{\operatorname{st}}),

respectively,
where
{\bf B}^{\leq r}_{\operatorname{st}}:=\oplus_{i=0}^{r}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}u^{i}_{\lambda}
,

u_{\lambda}=\log(\lambda)
, for fixed
\lambda
.

(2)

We set
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}):={\rm LMod}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\{\varphi,N\}}{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})
, where the ring
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\{\varphi,N\}
is defined as the abelian group
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}[\varphi,N]
with multiplication rules
\varphi a=\varphi(a)\varphi,Na-aN=N(a),N\varphi=p\varphi N
,
for
a\in{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
.

Proof.

The proof of the theorem runs over sections
2.3.2
(construction on the map)
and
2.3.3
(compatibility
with all structures).
∎

2.3.2.
Construction of the quasi-isomophism

\bullet

The index sets
.
Recall that we have assumed that one can find a finite extension
L/K
such that

\overline{f}
is the base change of
a fine log-scheme
{f}_{L}:Z_{1}\to\operatorname{Spec}({\mathscr{O}}_{L,1})^{\times}
, log-smooth and of Cartier type, by the natural map
\theta:\overline{S}_{1}\to\operatorname{Spec}({\mathscr{O}}_{L,1})^{\times}
.
That is, we have a map
\theta_{L}:X_{1}\to Z_{1}
such that the square
(\overline{f},{f}_{L},\theta,\theta_{L})
is Cartesian.
Such data
\Sigma:=\{(L,f_{L},\theta_{L})\}
clearly form a filtered set
16
16

16

In
[
20
, 4.3.1]
, in the case of a semistable formal scheme
{\mathscr{X}}
over
{\mathscr{O}}_{C}
, we have used a different index set
\Sigma
, call it
\Sigma^{\rm old}
. It is easy to see that we obtain the same theory with both choices of the index set: if
{\mathscr{X}}
is affine then the canonical map
\Sigma^{\rm old}\to\Sigma
makes
\Sigma^{\rm old}
cofinal in
\Sigma
.
.

We have similar data
\Sigma^{0}:=\{(\theta^{0},f^{0},\theta^{0}_{L})\}
:

(1)

\theta^{0}:\overline{S}^{0}\to S^{\prime,0}
is a map of log-schemes over
S^{0}_{K}
with
S^{\prime,0}=\operatorname{Spf}W(k^{\prime})
, where
k^{\prime}\subset\overline{k}
is finite over
k
and the log-structure of
S^{\prime,0}
is generated by one element; the Frobenius on the log-scheme
S^{\prime,0}
is induced, via the map
\theta^{0}
, from the Frobenius on
\overline{S}^{0}
;

(2)

f^{0}:Z^{0}_{1}\to S^{\prime,0}_{1}
is log-smooth, fine and integral, of Cartier type;

(3)

\theta^{0}_{X}:X^{0}_{1}\to Z^{0}_{1}
is such that the square
(\overline{f}^{0},f^{0},\theta^{0}_{1},\theta^{0}_{X})
is Cartesian.

Such data again form a filtered set. There is a map of filtered sets
\Sigma\to\Sigma^{0},Z_{1}/S_{L}\mapsto Z^{0}_{1}/S^{0}_{L}
; it is cofinal. These filtered sets are clearly functorial with respect to the maps
\overline{f}
.

Before proceeding let us make the following remark.

Remark 2.28
.

Let
M
be a complex equipped with an
N
-action.
Let
M^{N{\text{-nilp}}}:=[M\to M[N^{-1}]]
, where

M[N^{-1}]:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(M\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{N}}\,M\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{N}}\,\cdots).

For

, we have strict quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

(2.29)

M_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}(M_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N{\text{-nilp}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(M_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{l,\operatorname{st}})^{N{\text{-nilp}}}\simeq(M\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(\overline{k})}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st}})^{N{\text{-nilp}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}.

The last quasi-isomorphism in (
2.29

is built from torsion-free and

-complete modules. The previous two quasi-isomorphisms are clear algebraically because we can assume that

is globally nilpotent on

); it is also clear topologically because

is built from Banach spaces and

is a Banach space (so the derived tensor product is given by the tensor product itself).

Similarly, for

(note that now the action of

{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

-linearization in

\bullet

\pi

\xi^{0}
.

18

18

Up to a contractible set of choices, of course.
of

, we note that, for

, there are natural compatible with Frobenius transition maps

. Then the

To show that

. This map is clearly functorial with respect to
X_{1}
.

2.3.3.
Compatibility of the arithmetic and geometric maps
{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{HK}}
.

It remains to prove that the map
{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}
is a quasi-isomorphism and that the last claim of our corollary holds. For that, assume that

comes from

. Choose

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(Z_{1}/R)_{l}{\otimes}^{\rm L}_{R}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/(\overline{S}_{1},{\mathscr{E}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}){\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(\overline{k})}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st},n}.

By applying

we remove

. Composing with the

\widehat{\varepsilon}_{\operatorname{st},l}:({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{1}^{0})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(\overline{k})}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st}})_{{\bf Q}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{W(\overline{k})}\widehat{{\bf A}}_{l,\operatorname{st}})_{{\bf Q}}

Denote by

{\varepsilon}_{\operatorname{st},l}^{\operatorname{HK}}:=\kappa_{l}^{-1}(\widehat{\varepsilon}_{\operatorname{st},l})^{N{\text{-nilp}}}\kappa_{l}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}

the associated map in

. It is a strict quasi-isomorphism.
Now, choose

large enough so that the action of

on

factors as
Z_{1}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{F_{m}}}\,Z^{0}_{1}\hookrightarrow Z_{1}
. Take

. It is easy to see, using the uniqueness statement from Theorem

{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st},\xi^{0},\pi}
equals

. In particular, the map
{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}
is a strict quasi-isomorphism, as wanted.

The final claim of the theorem
follows since
{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st},\lambda}=s^{*}_{\lambda}{\varepsilon}_{\operatorname{st},l}^{\operatorname{HK}}.

2.3.4.
Comparison between Hyodo-Kato and de Rham cohomologies

Theorem
2.22
implies the following Hyodo-Kato-to-de Rham quasi-isomorphisms:

Corollary 2.31
.

We have natural strict quasi-isomorphisms

(2.32)

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\quad\mbox{ in }{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C}),

\displaystyle{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{HK}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\quad\mbox{ in }{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}).

They are compatible via the maps
\theta:{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to C
and
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/\overline{S})
.

Proof.

From Theorem
2.22

we have a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})

(2.33)

\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}.

Take the map
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
given by sending
\log(\lambda_{p})\mapsto 0
. It is not Galois equivariant but this will not be a problem for us. Applying it to the quasi-isomorphism (
2.33
), which is
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
-linear, we get a strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}})

(2.34)

\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{cr}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}.

We tensor it now over
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
with C. By Lemma
2.2
,
we obtain the strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})

(2.35)

\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}C

and, composing with the strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}},

the quasi-isomorphism
\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{dR}}
from our corollary.
We note that
\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is compatible with the Galois action
because
\sigma(\log(\lambda_{p}))-\log(\lambda_{p})\in{\rm Ker}\,\theta
.

Proceeding as above we get
the strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}})

(2.36)

\tilde{\varepsilon}^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{dR}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{i}),\quad i\geq 0.

Taking
{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{i}
of both sides gives us now the second strict quasi-isomorphism of the theorem.
∎

3.
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology

This section is devoted to the definitions of rigid analytic
and overconvergent
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomologies
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
, for
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}

or
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
, and to the study of their basic properties.
These cohomologies are replacements for
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{C}^{\mathrm{R}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
which does not
exist since there is no continuous ring morphism
C\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
although
\overline{K}
is naturally
a subring of
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
: if
X
is defined over
K
, then

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{\mathrm{R}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
.
In general, we have the relation
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf C}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X)

(see Proposition
3.13

and Proposition
3.29
for this comparison and analogous results concerning filtrations).

In the next chapter, using the Hyodo-Kato map, we will prove that,
if
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
is partially proper, then the rigid analytic
and overconvergent
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomologies give the same result:
if
X^{\dagger}
is the associated dagger variety,
the natural map
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}(X^{\dagger}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm dR}({X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is a
strict quasi-isomorphism (Corollary
4.32
).

Our rigid analytic
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology is defined by, locally, Hodge-completing absolute crystalline cohomology, but it gives
the same object (see Proposition
3.27
)
as the constructions of Bhatt-Morrow-Scholze
[
10
]
and Guo
[
30
]
via
the infinitesimal site.

3.1.
CliffsNotes

For a quick reference, we will recall now some results from
[
20
]
and add few complements.
