Remark 3.2
.

(i) Below, in Section
6.2.2
, we will define the overconvergent period map in 1d over
C
and, in Proposition
6.8
, we will remove the condition
L=K
in 3c.
To do this we could not use the constructions from
[
17
]
and
[
20
]
: the first one was not functorial enough, the second one, using a “killing nilpotents” trick,
just did not transfer to the geometric setting.
This depressing state of affairs
made us take a break of more than a year from the project before coming back to it with an
approach that adapts to the analytic setting
an early construction by Beilinson of the Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphism.

(ii) The local-global compatibility for rigid analytic geometric syntomic cohomology also holds. This will be proved in Proposition
5.3
using local-global compatibility for Hyodo-Kato and
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomologies.

3.1.2.
Complements

Now we pass to complementary results.

(1)
\eta
-étale descent.
The following proposition should have been included in
[
20
]
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
({\mathscr{B}},F)
be a Beilinson base
24
24

24

Such a base was introduced by Beilinson in
[
2
, 2.1]
; it is a slightly more general notion than that of a Verdier base which is commonly used.
of an essentially small site
{\mathscr{V}}
. Then:

(1)

The functor
F:{\mathscr{B}}\to{\mathscr{V}}
is continuous.

(2)

F
induces an equivalence of topoi

{\rm Sh}({\mathscr{B}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm Sh}({\mathscr{V}}).

(3)

Let
{\mathscr{D}}
be a presentable
\infty
-category. Then
F
induces an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

{\rm Sh}^{\rm hyp}({\mathscr{B}},{\mathscr{D}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm Sh}^{\rm hyp}({\mathscr{V}},{\mathscr{D}})

of hypersheaves.

Proof.

Claims (1) and (2) were shown in the proof of
[
20
, Prop. 2.2]
.
For claim (3), recall that, for a site
{\mathscr{C}}
, the
\infty
-category of hypersheaves is defined as

{\rm Sh}^{\rm hyp}({\mathscr{C}},{\mathscr{D}}):={\rm Sh}^{\rm hyp}({\mathscr{C}},{\rm Ani})\otimes{\mathscr{D}},

where
{\rm Ani}
is the
\infty
-category of anima and
\otimes

denotes the tensor product of
\infty
-categories
[
37
, Sec. 4.8.1]
. Hence it suffices to prove claim (3) for the
\infty
-category of anima and in that case it follows easily from (2) and the fact that the Brown-Joyal-Jardine model structure on simplicial presheaves presents the
\infty
-topos of hypercomplete sheaves (see
[
36
, Prop. 6.5.2.14]
).
∎

Remark 3.4
.

To lighten up the terminology, in the rest of the paper we will call "hypersheaves" "sheaves" and a "hypersheafification" a "sheafification".

Remark 3.5
.

The example most relevant for this paper is the following:
{\mathscr{V}}={\rm Sm}_{C,{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}}
,
the site of smooth rigid analytic varieties over
C
equipped with the étale topology.
{\mathscr{V}}
has a Beilinson base
({\mathscr{M}},F_{\eta})
, where
{\mathscr{M}}
is the category of basic semistable formal models

{\mathscr{M}}^{{\rm ss},b}_{C}
or semistable formal models
{\mathscr{M}}^{\rm ss}_{C}
and
F_{\eta}
is the forgetful functor
{\mathscr{X}}\to{\mathscr{X}}_{\eta}
from formal schemes to their rigid analytic generic fibers (see
[
20
, Prop. 2.8]
). We have similar constructions for the site
{\mathscr{V}}={\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C,\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}
of smooth dagger varieties over
C
with the corresponding categories
{\mathscr{M}}^{\dagger,{\rm ss},b}_{C}
and
{\mathscr{M}}^{\dagger,\rm ss}_{C}
of basic semistable and semistable weak formal models.

If
{\mathscr{F}}\in{\mathscr{D}}
, for a presentable
\infty
-category
{\mathscr{D}}
, is a presheaf on a Beilinson base
{\mathscr{B}}
, then the presheaf on
{\mathscr{V}}
defined by

U\mapsto({\mathscr{F}}^{a}(U):=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}{\mathscr{F}}(V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})),

where the colimit is taken over hypercoverings
25
25

25

Here and below, to simplify notation, we write
{\mathscr{F}}(V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
for
\lim{\mathscr{F}}(V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
.

V_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to U
from
{\mathscr{B}}
,
defines a hypersheaf on
{\mathscr{V}}
. In the context of the above example of a Beilinson base we call it
\eta
-étale descent of
{\mathscr{F}}
.

(2) The following corollary removes the condition
L=K
in 3b of Proposition
3.1
and could have been included in
[
20
]
.

Corollary 3.6
.

Let
X
be a smooth partially proper dagger variety over
C
and let
\widehat{X}
denote its completion. Let
W
be a Fréchet space over
\breve{F}
. Then the natural map
26
26

26

See the point (4) below for the reminder on the definition of the tensor products used.
in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\breve{F}})

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Find an admissible covering of
X
by dagger affinoids and then look at the set of their naive interiors (a
naive interior
of a smooth dagger affinoid is a Stein subvariety whose complement is open and quasi-compact
27
27

27

We have an analogous definition of a naive interior of a rigid analytic affinoid.
). By the definition of partially proper dagger varieties this is an admissible covering of
X
as well. The individual varieties in the covering are partially proper and, moreover, are defined over a finite extension of
K
. The latter fact is true because the corresponding rigid affinoids are defined over a finite extension of
K

by Elkik’s theorem
[
22
, Th. 7, Rem. 2]
(the finite presentation condition there is satisfied in our case by the finiteness theorems of Grauert-Remmert-Gruson and Gruson-Raynaud
[
38
, Th. 3.1.17, Th. 3.2.1]
). Same can be said about the intersections of a finite number of them.

Now, taking the associated Čech cover and evaluating on it the morphism from the corollary we get a strict quasi-isomorphism by point 3b of Proposition
3.1
. We conclude by rigid analytic descent.
∎

(3) We will recall now a result from
[
20
]
together with a new proof (since the proof supplied in loc. cit. is a bit sketchy). This proof will serve us as a template for proofs of analogous claims.

Proposition 3.7
.

(Local-global compatibility,
[
20
, Prop. 4.23]
)

Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{\operatorname{ss},b}_{C}
.
The natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to show that, for any
\eta
-étale hypercovering
{\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
of
{\mathscr{X}}
from
{\mathscr{M}}^{\operatorname{ss},b}_{C}
, the natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},1})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism (modulo taking a refinement of
{\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
). We may assume that in every degree of the hypercovering we have a finite number of formal models. Passing to cohomology (
\widetilde{H}(-)
-cohomology) and then to a truncated hypercovering we can assume that all the formal schemes mod
p
and maps between them that are involved are defined over a common field
L
(we will denote them with subscript
{\mathscr{O}}_{L}
), a finite extension of
K
. We may leave that way the category of semistable models but we will still be in the category of log-smooth models (with Cartier type reduction).
We are reduced to showing that the map

(3.8)

\alpha:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L},1}/S_{L})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv}}({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},{\mathscr{O}}_{L},1}/S_{L})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Tensoring both sides of (
3.8
) with
C
over
L
we obtain a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L},1}/S_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{C}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv}}({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}},1}/S_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},C}).}

Since the bottom map is a
strict quasi-isomorphism (by étale descent) so is the top map
\alpha_{C}
. We claim that this, in turn, implies that the map
\alpha
itself is a strict-quasi-isomorphism. Indeed, passing to fibers of the horizontal arrows in the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L},1}/S_{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv}}({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}},1}/S_{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L},1}/S_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{C}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv}}({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}},1}/S_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}C,}

we see that it suffices to prove the following claim:

if
A\in{\mathscr{D}}(C_{L})
is a complex such that
A\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}C
is strictly acyclic then
A
is strictly acyclic as well.

To show this, write
C\simeq L\oplus W
, for a Banach space
W\in C_{L}
, and conclude.
∎

(4) Let
X
be a smooth rigid analytic variety. In
[
20
]
, we have considered a number of nonstandard tensor products. For example,
we have defined (see
[
20
, 4.21]
) in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})
,
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})
:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\overline{K}}:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\overline{K}})({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},1})),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}C:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{C})({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},1})),

where the homotopy colimit is taken over affinoid
\eta
-étale hypercoverings
{\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
from
{\mathscr{M}}^{\operatorname{ss},b}_{C}
. These tensor products satisfy local-global compatibility. A fact that can be proved as in the following example:

Proposition 3.9
.

(Local-global compatibility for tensor products)
Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{\operatorname{ss},b}_{C}
. The canonical maps in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})
,
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\overline{K}}\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\overline{K}},

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}C\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}C

are strict quasi-isomorphisms.

Proof.

For the second morphism, proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
3.7
and using its notation, we reduce to showing that the canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L},1})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F_{L}}C\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},{\mathscr{O}}_{L},1})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F_{L}}C

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. But this is clear since, via the Hyodo-Kato morphism, this map is strictly quasi-isomorphic to the map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},C}),

which is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

For the first morphism, we proceed in the same way ending up with the strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(L)

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},L}).

Passing to homotopy colimit over finite extensions of
L
in
\overline{K}
, we finish the argument.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

(1) The local-global compatibility of nonstandard tensor products (see
[
20
, 5.16]
) also holds for the dagger varieties and the Grosse-Klönne Hyodo-Kato cohomology. The proof of this fact is a simple analog of the proof of Proposition
3.9
.

(2) In Proposition
3.9
we can replace
C
with any Fréchet space
B
over
\breve{F}
. This requires just a slight modification of the proof: pass from
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(-)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F_{L}}B
to

({\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(-)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F_{L}}C)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F_{L}}B
,
use the Hyodo-Kato morphism to pass to de Rham cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}((-)_{C})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F_{L}}B
, use étale descent for de Rham cohomology, and go back to
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(-)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F_{L}}B
via
C\simeq F_{L}\oplus W
.

3.2.
Geometric crystalline cohomology

Our rigid analytic
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology will be defined locally as a completion of the absolute crystalline cohomology. We will start then by recalling the definition of the latter.

3.2.1.
Relative crystalline cohomology

Let
{\overline{f}}:X_{1}\to\overline{S}_{1}
be a map of log-schemes, with integral quasi-coherent source.
Suppose that
\overline{f}
is the base change of
a fine log-smooth log-scheme
{f}_{L}:Z_{1}\to S_{L,1}
, by the natural map
\theta:\overline{S}_{1}\to S_{L,1}
, for a finite extension
L/K
.
That is, we have a map
\theta_{L}:X_{1}\to Z_{1}
such that the square
(\overline{f},{f}_{L},\theta,\theta_{L})
is Cartesian.
Such data
\Sigma_{1}:=\{(L,f_{L},\theta_{L})\}
form a filtered set.

(a)
C
-version.
Let
{\mathscr{A}}^{\rm rel}_{\operatorname{cr}}

be the
\eta
-étale sheafification of the presheaf

{\mathscr{X}}\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
on
{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\operatorname{ss},b}
. Note that
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\in{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{C})
. For
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
, we
set
28
28

28

Here we think of
X
as an
\eta
-étale sheaf on
{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\operatorname{ss},b}
.
in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{C})

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm rel}_{\operatorname{cr}}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}^{\rm rel}_{\operatorname{cr}}).

It is a filtered dg
C
-algebra equipped with a continuous action of

{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
. It is equipped with the topology induced from the topology of the
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})
’s. Since the models
{\mathscr{X}}
are log-smooth over
\overline{S}
, we have natural (strict)
quasi-isomorphisms (the first one in the category of sheaves with values in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{C})
, the second one – in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{C})
).

(3.11)

{\mathscr{A}}^{\rm rel}_{\operatorname{cr}}\simeq{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{dR}},\quad{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm rel}_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

(b)
\overline{K}
-version.
Let
{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}

be the
\eta
-étale sheafification of the presheaf

{\mathscr{X}}\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{X}})
on
{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\operatorname{ss},b}
, where we
set in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{X}}):=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{\Sigma_{1}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(Z_{1}/S_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}.

For
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
, we set
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}})
in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})
. It is a dg
\overline{K}
-algebra equipped with a continuous action of

{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
(this action is smooth if
X
is quasi-compact). It is equipped with the topology induced from the topology of the
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(Z_{1}/S_{L})
’s.
There are natural continuous morphisms (the first one in the category of sheaves with values in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})
, the second one – in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})
)

{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}\to{\mathscr{A}}^{\rm rel}_{\operatorname{cr}},\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm rel}_{\operatorname{cr}}(X).

Lemma 3.12
.

(1)

(
Local-global compatibility
)
Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{{\rm ss},b}_{C}
.
The natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{X}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

(
Product formula
)
For
X\in{\rm Sm}_{K}
, the natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\overline{K}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}(X_{C})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Since, for
{\mathscr{Y}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{{\rm ss},b}_{C}
, the natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{Y}})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}({\mathscr{Y}})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism, it induces a strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\overline{K}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{conv},\overline{K}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr},\overline{K}}(X),\quad X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}.

Hence
our lemma follows from analogous claims for convergent
\overline{K}
-cohomology which are known (see
[
20
, the proof of Prop. 4.23]
or Proposition
3.7
).
∎

3.2.2.
Absolute crystalline cohomology

Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{\rm ss}_{C}
.
Recall that we have the absolute crystalline cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\in{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}_{\varphi}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}})
equipped with the Hodge filtration
F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}:={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
, for
r\geq 0
.
Let
{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}
and
F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}
,
r\geq 0
,
be the
\eta
-étale sheafifications of the presheaves

{\mathscr{X}}\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
and
{\mathscr{X}}\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
, respectively, on
{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\operatorname{ss}}
.
For
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
, we set in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}_{\varphi}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}),\quad F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}),\quad r\geq 0.

It is a dg filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
-algebra equipped with a continuous action of

{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
.
It is equipped with the topology induced from the topology of the
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
’s.

The local-global comparison requires the Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphism and will be proven in Lemma
5.2
below (just the nonfiltered case).

3.3.
Rigid analytic
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology

We will define now rigid analytic
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology, list its basic properties, and compare it with already existing definitions.

3.3.1.
Definition of rigid analytic
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology

Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{\rm ss}_{C}
. To define rigid analytic
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology, we start with the absolute crystalline cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
and complete it with respect to the Hodge filtration
F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
,
r\geq 0
:

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}:={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}/F^{r}),\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}:={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{j\geq r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}/F^{j}).

This is a dg filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-algebra, hence a complex in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
.
The corresponding
\eta
-étale sheafifications on
{\mathscr{M}}^{\rm ss}_{C}
we will denote by

F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr}}
,
r\geq 0
. We have canonical maps

\kappa:F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}\to F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr}}
,
r\geq 0
. Moreover, the canonical
map
\vartheta:F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
, compatible with the map
\theta:{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\to C
, extends to a map

\vartheta:F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
, which, in turn, globalizes to a map

\vartheta:F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}\to F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\rm rel}_{\operatorname{cr}}
.

For
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
, define the

{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr}}),\quad F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr}}),\quad r\geq 0.

This is a dg filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-algebra, equipped with a continuous action of

{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
. It is equipped with the topology induced from the topology of the
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
’s.

The local-global comparison requires product formula and will be proven in Lemma
3.23
below.

We have canonical maps (the first one in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
, the second one – in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
)

\displaystyle\kappa:\quad

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),

\displaystyle\vartheta:\quad

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),\quad r\geq 0.

It is immediate from the definitions that the first map
yields a strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\kappa:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),

where we set
{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}:={\mathrm{R}}\lim_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)/F^{r})
in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
.

3.3.2.
Comparison results.

(1) We start with a comparison of
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
- and de Rham cohomologies.

(i)
Projection from
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology to de Rham cohomology
.

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
.

(1)

We have a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{C})

\vartheta:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

(2)

More generally, for
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

(3.14)

F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)

(3)

For
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished
triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

(3.15)

F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta_{X}}}{{\to}}\bigoplus_{i\leq r}{\rm R}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{i}_{X})(r-i)[-i]

Proof.

In the first claim, the tensor product is simply defined as the cofiber of the map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Hence it suffices to show that we have the distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

Étale locally this translates into the triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}},

where
{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}_{L,C}
, for a semistable affine model
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}
over a finite extension
L
of
K
.

This triangle
fits into a commutative diagram:

(3.16)

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes t}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}}

\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

Here the vertical
maps

\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}:={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}\iota_{{\rm BK},r}
, with
\iota_{{\rm BK},r}
defined as the composition

(3.17)

\displaystyle\iota_{{\rm BK},r}:\quad({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/S_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(\overline{S}/S_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}})/F^{r}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\cup}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/S_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}/F^{r}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}/F^{r},

where we set

(3.18)

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}_{L}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}F^{r}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\Omega^{1}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}F^{r-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\cdots).

The first quasi-isomorphism in (
3.17
) follows from the fact that
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}
is log-smooth over
{\mathscr{O}}_{L}
and that, more generally, derived de Rham complex computes crystalline cohomology for log-syntomic schemes (both Hodge completed) by
[
4
, 1.9.2]
. The second quasi-isomorphism is just log-smooth base change for crystalline cohomology (more explicitly, one can proceed as in
[
47
, Prop. 4.5.4]
). And the third quasi-isomorphism is a formal scheme version of
[
41
, Cor. 2.4]
(the proof in
loc. cit.
goes through in our setting).

The bottom row in diagram (
3.16
) is a distinguished triangle. It follows that the top row in our diagram is a distinguished triangle as well, as wanted.

The second claim, étale locally, reduces to showing that the triangle

F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}},

where
{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}_{L,C}
, for a semistable affine model
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}
over a finite extension
L
of
K
, is distinguished.
This triangle
fits into a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}
\textstyle{F^{r-1}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes t}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}}
\textstyle{F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\theta}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}}
\textstyle{F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

The bottom row is a distinguished triangle: use the expression (
3.18
) to reduce to showing that, for
r-1\geq i\geq 0
,
we have a strict quasi-isomorphism

t:\Omega^{i}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}F^{r-1-i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Omega^{i}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}F^{r-i}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

and the triangle

\Omega^{r}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,\Omega^{r}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\Omega^{r}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}C

is distinguished. But the first claim is clear and the second claim was just proved in (1).

The third claim, étale locally, reduces to identifying the graded term in the distinguished triangle

F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\to\operatorname{gr}^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}},

where
{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}_{L,C}
, for a semistable affine model
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}
over a finite extension
L
of
K
. That is, we want to show that

\operatorname{gr}^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\simeq\bigoplus_{i\leq r}\Omega^{i}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}t^{r-i}C[-i].

The above triangle
fits into a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{gr}^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}}
\textstyle{F^{r+1}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}}
\textstyle{F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{gr}^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{f_{{\mathscr{X}}}}

Using expression (
3.18
) we get

\displaystyle\operatorname{gr}^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}_{L}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{g_{{\mathscr{X}}}}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}t^{r}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 0}}{{\to}}\Omega^{1}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}t^{r-1}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 0}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 0}}{{\to}}\Omega^{r}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}t^{0}C)

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{i\leq r}\Omega^{i}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}t^{r-i}C[-i],

as wanted. The global map
\beta_{X}
is defined by globalizing the local maps
\beta_{{\mathscr{X}}}:=g_{{\mathscr{X}}}f^{-1}_{{\mathscr{X}}}
.
∎

Remark 3.19
.

(1) The above proof shows that we have a distinguished triangle

{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,{\mathscr{A}}^{\rm rel}_{\operatorname{cr}}.

(2) The maps
\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}
above can be defined in a more general set-up, where
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}
is assumed to be just log-syntomic over
S_{L}
. It is again a strict quasi-isomorphism and the proof of this claim is not much different than in the log-smooth case: The fact that the second map
in the definition of
\iota_{{\rm BK},r}
in (
3.17
) is a strict quasi-isomorphism can be seen by unwinding both sides of the cup product map: one finds a Künneth morphism for certain de Rham complexes. It is an integral quasi-isomorphism because these complexes are "flat enough" which follows from the fact that the maps
{\mathscr{O}}_{C,n}\to{\mathscr{O}}_{L,n}
and
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L},n}\to{\mathscr{O}}_{L,n}
, for
n\geq 0
,
are log-syntomic (see the proof of
[
47
, Prop. 4.5.4]
for a similar argument). The third map in (
3.17
) is a strict quasi-isomorphism (integrally, a quasi-isomorphism up to a constant dependent on
L
) by an argument analogous to the one given in the proof of
[
41
, Cor. 2.4]
.

(ii)
Product formula
. Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{K}
. The morphisms
\hat{\iota}_{{\rm BK}}
from Remark
3.19

induce a
morphism
29
29

29

See the proof of Lemma
3.20
for details.
in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\iota_{\rm BK}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Lemma 3.20
.

The morphism
\iota_{\rm BK}

is a strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
.

Remark 3.21
.

The filtration on
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is defined by the formula

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})(U_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})),

where the homotopy colimit is taken over étale affinoid hypercoverings
U_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
of
X
and, for an affinoid
U
,

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(U)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}({\mathscr{O}}(U)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}F^{r}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\Omega^{1}(U)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}F^{r-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\cdots).

Since
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)
satisfies filtered étale descent
30
30

30

Use that the varieties are smooth and we have étale descent for the structure sheaf.
, it is easy to see that so does
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
.

Proof.

We may argue étale locally and assume that
X={\mathscr{X}}_{L}
, for a semistable affine model
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}
, for a finite extension
L
of
K
. Then
X_{C}={\mathscr{X}}_{L}\times_{K}L
. We need to show that the map

\iota_{\rm BK}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/S_{K})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\hat{\iota}_{\rm BK}}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}\times_{{\mathscr{O}}_{K}}{{\mathscr{O}}_{C}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}

is a filtered strict quasi-isomorphism. For that, since the base change map

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/S_{K})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/S_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}

is a filtered strict quasi-isomorphism,
it suffices to show that so is
the canonical map

(3.22)

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/S_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}{\to}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/S_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}.

But we can write (the differentials are over
L
):

\displaystyle F^{j}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/S_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}F^{j}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\Omega^{1}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}F^{j-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\cdots)

\displaystyle F^{j}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}/S_{L})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}

\displaystyle={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}(F^{j}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r})\to\Omega^{1}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}(F^{j-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-1})\to\cdots).

And now we can argue degreewise. But then, for
s\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}(\Omega^{i}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}(F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r}))\simeq\Omega^{i}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}(F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r})\simeq\Omega^{i}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}.

The second quasi-isomorphism follows from the fact that we have
F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r}\simeq C^{r-s}
as topological
K
-vector spaces and the maps
F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r+i}\to F^{s}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r}
,
i\geq 0
, are surjective.
This finishes the proof.

∎

(2) Now we pass to comparisons between
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology and crystalline cohomology.

Lemma 3.23
.

(Local-global compatibility)

Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{{\rm ss},b}_{C}
and let
r\geq 0
. The canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\kappa:\quad F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}{\to}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

We may argue étale locally on
{\mathscr{X}}
. Assume thus that
{\mathscr{X}}\simeq{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L},{\mathscr{O}}_{C}}
, for a semistable affine model
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}}
over
S_{L}
,
[L:K]<\infty
.
From the product quasi-isomorphisms from Lemma
3.20
and its proof (where we took
L=K
) we
get the commutative diagram

\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}
\textstyle{F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{L}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm BK}}
\textstyle{F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm BK}}

and the two vertical strict quasi-isomorphisms. The bottom horizontal
strict quasi-isomorphism follows from the local-global property of
F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{L})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
(see Remark
3.21
).
∎

Lemma 3.24
.

The canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\displaystyle\kappa\otimes 1:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),\quad r\geq 0,

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Here, we set

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})),

where the homotopy colimit is taken over
\eta
-étale quasi-compact hypercoverings
{\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
from
{\mathscr{M}}^{\operatorname{ss},b}_{C}
(that is, hypercoverings
{\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
such that every
{\mathscr{U}}_{n}
,
n\geq 0
, is quasi-compact).

Proof.

It suffices to show that, for an affine
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{{\rm ss},b}_{C}
, the canonical map

{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{r})){\to}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}/F^{r})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. Take a log-smooth lifting

{\mathscr{Y}}
of
{\mathscr{X}}
over
\operatorname{Spf}({\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}})
. We have

(3.25)

\displaystyle({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{r})

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{r})\to\Omega^{1}_{{\mathscr{Y}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}},{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{r})\to\cdots),

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}/F^{r}

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{r})\to\Omega^{1}_{{\mathscr{Y}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}},{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{r-1})\to\cdots)

The claim in the lemma is now clear.
∎

3.3.3.
History

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
.

(i) Recall that Bhatt-Morrow-Scholze in
[
10
, Sec. 13]
introduced
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology of
X
, which they call
31
31

31

We take here the étale version studied in
[
13
, Sec. 6.2]
and not the original analytic version. The two versions are quasi-isomorphic by
[
13
, Sec. 6.2]
.

crystalline cohomology of
X
over
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
.
We will denote it by
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm BMS}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
and see as a complex in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
.
As they mention
[
10
, Rem. 13.2]
, morally speaking, it is the infinitesimal cohomology of
X
over the embedding given by the map
\theta:{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to C
. It is defined though in such a way that it is easy to compare it with
{\bf A}_{\rm inf}
-cohomology. Similarly here, we have defined
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
in such a way that it is easy to compare it with crystalline cohomology over
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}
.

(ii) The infinitesimal site definition of
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm BMS}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
was carried out by Guo in
[
30
, Sec. 7.2]
(see also
[
31
]
). We will denote this version of
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-chomology by

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\in{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
(
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm inf}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
).
It comes equipped with a Hodge filtration (which was ignored in
[
10
]
). Moreover, Guo constructed a natural quasi-isomorphism (see
[
30
, Cor. 1.2.9., Th. 1.2.7]
)

(3.26)

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm BMS}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

(iii) Our construction of
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology is compatible with the above constructions:

Proposition 3.27
.

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
.

(1)

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm BMS}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

(2)

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Proof.

Claim (1) follows from claim (2) and the quasi-isomorphism (
3.26
).

To prove claim (2), recall that
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is defined by taking, étale locally, the Hodge completed absolute crystalline cohomology and then globalizing. More specifically, let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{\rm ss}_{C}
. We have

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}:={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}/F^{r}),

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}:={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{j\geq r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}/F^{j}).

On the other hand
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is defined as the infinitesimal cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm inf}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
equipped with its natural Hodge filtration. It satifies étale descent.

This means that,
if
{\mathscr{X}}
is affine and (exactly and) closely embedded in an affine formal log-scheme
{\mathscr{Y}}
, log-smooth over
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}
, then in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}(({\mathscr{D}}_{{\mathscr{X}}}({\mathscr{Y}})/F^{r})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\to(({\mathscr{D}}_{{\mathscr{X}}}({\mathscr{Y}})/F^{r-1})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}})}\Omega^{1}_{{\mathscr{Y}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\to\cdots),

where
{\mathscr{D}}_{{\mathscr{X}}}({\mathscr{Y}})
is the PD-envelope of
{\mathscr{X}}
in
{\mathscr{Y}}
and the tensor product is
p
-adic.
On the other hand, we have in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm inf}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}({\mathscr{D}}_{{\mathscr{X}}_{C}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{C})/F^{r}\to({\mathscr{D}}_{{\mathscr{X}}_{C}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{C})/F^{r-1})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{C})}\Omega^{1}_{{\mathscr{Y}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}\to\cdots),

where
{\mathscr{D}}_{{\mathscr{X}}_{C}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{{\bf B}})
is the inf-envelope of
{\mathscr{X}}_{C}
in
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B}:={\mathscr{Y}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}
. Since
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},{\bf Q}_{p}}/F^{i}\simeq{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{i}
, we have a natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\rm inf}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}.

This can be globalized to a map in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}.

We claim that it is a strict quasi-isomorphism. Indeed, it suffices to show this locally so we may assume that we have the data of integral models
{\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{Y}}
as above and, moreover,
{\mathscr{Y}}
is a lifting of
{\mathscr{X}}
. Then

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})/F^{r}\to(({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}})/F^{r-1})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}})}\Omega^{1}_{{\mathscr{Y}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\to\cdots),

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\rm Guo}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\simeq{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})/F^{r}\to({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})/F^{r-1})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})}\Omega^{1}_{{\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}\to\cdots).

But we have the topological isomorphisms

\displaystyle(({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}})/F^{i})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}})}\Omega^{j}_{{\mathscr{Y}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})/F^{i})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})}\Omega^{j}_{{\mathscr{Y}}_{{\bf B},i}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},{\bf Q}_{p},i}}

\displaystyle({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})/F^{i})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})}\Omega^{j}_{{\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})/F^{i})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}({\mathscr{Y}}_{\bf B})}\Omega^{j}_{{\mathscr{Y}}_{{\bf B},i}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR},i}},

where
(-)_{i}
denotes moding out by
F^{i}
. Hence the strict quasi-isomorphism

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r},

as wanted.

Having the strict quasi-isomorphism

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r},

we may take
{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}
of both sides to obtain the strict quasi-isomorphism

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

This is because we have

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}),\quad{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r})

as can be easily seen by a computation similar to the one used in the proof of Lemma
3.20
.
Finally, to obtain the strict quasi-isomorphism

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm Guo}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),\quad r\geq 0,

we use the distinguished triangles

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r}

for both cohomologies.
∎

3.4.
Overconvergent
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology

We define overconvergent
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology via presentations of dagger structures.

3.4.1.
Definition of overconvergent
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology

Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
. Let
{\rm pres}(X)=\{X_{h}\}
be a presentation of
X
(see
[
20
, Sec. 3.2.1]
for relevant definitions).
Define in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),\quad r\geq 0.

For
r\geq 0
, the étale sheafification
32
32

32

See
[
48
, Def. 2.1]
for the definition of étale topology of dagger varieties.
of
F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
on
{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
gives us a sheaf
F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr}}
. The filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
of a smooth dagger variety
X
over
C

is defined as

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{\operatorname{cr}}),\quad r\geq 0.

Remark 3.28
.

If
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
the above two definitions of
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
and
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
agree. This will be shown in Corollary
4.28
below by reduction to Hyodo-Kato cohomology via the Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphism.

3.4.2.
Properties of overconvergent
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology

We will now prove properties of overconvergent
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology that do not require Hyodo-Kato cohomology.

We have canonical maps in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
and
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{K})

\displaystyle\vartheta:\quad

\displaystyle F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),\quad X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C},r\geq 0,

\displaystyle\iota_{\rm BK}:\quad

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),\quad X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{K},

induced by their rigid analytic analogs.

Proposition 3.29
.

(1)

(Projection)
Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
.

(a)

The map
\vartheta
defined above yields a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{C})

\vartheta:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

(b)

More generally, for
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

(3.30)

F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)

(c)

For
r\geq 0
, we have a natural distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

(3.31)

F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to\bigoplus_{i\leq r}{\rm R}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{i}_{X})(r-i)[-i]

(2)

(Product formula)
Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{K}
. The map
\iota_{\rm BK}

defined above yields a natural quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\iota_{\rm BK}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

See Remark
3.32
below for the definition of the tensor product.

(3)

(
t
-completeness)
The canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r}))

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Remark 3.32
.

In Proposition
3.29
(2),
the filtration on
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is defined by the formula

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})(U_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})),

where the homotopy colimit is taken over étale dagger affinoid hypercoverings
U_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
of
X
and, for a smooth dagger affinoid
U
,

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(U)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}):={\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}({\mathscr{O}}(U)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}F^{r}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\Omega^{1}(U)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}F^{r-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\cdots).

In particular, if
r=0
,

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(U)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is just the usual projective tensor product.

Proof.

To prove the first projection formula in (1) it suffices to argue locally for the dagger cohomologies. So we may assume that
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid with the presentation
\{X_{h}\}
. We need to show that
the projection

\vartheta:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\dagger}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. We can write this projection more explicitly as the composition

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\dagger}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}C

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}C

\displaystyle\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}\vartheta}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

The second map is a strict quasi-isomorphism because the tensor product is defined as the cone of multiplication by
t
; the third map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Proposition
3.13
.

To prove the second formula in (1), we argue locally as well. We need to show that, for
r\geq 0
, we have a distinguished triangle

(3.33)

F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),

where
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid with the presentation
\{X_{h}\}
. But this triangle can be written as:

\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}t}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}\vartheta}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})

and then it is clear that it is distinguished by Proposition
3.13
.

To prove the third formula in (1), we again argue locally. We need to show that, for
r\geq 0
, we have a distinguished triangle

(3.34)

F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{can}}}{{\to}}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\beta_{X}}}{{\to}}\bigoplus_{i\leq r}{\rm R}\Gamma(X,\Omega^{i}_{X})(r-i)[-i],

where
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid with the presentation
\{X_{h}\}
.
But we can define this triangle as:

\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r+1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}\bigoplus_{i\leq r}{\rm R}\Gamma(X_{h},\Omega^{i}_{X_{h}})(r-i)[-i]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{[\,1\,]}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}\beta_{X_{h}}}

and then it is clear that it is distinguished by Proposition
3.13
.

In the product formula (2), the map
\iota_{\rm BK}
is defined by globalizing maps
\iota^{\dagger}_{\rm BK}
for dagger affinoids. To define the latter,
assume that
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid with the presentation
\{X_{h}\}
and
set

(3.35)

\displaystyle\iota^{\dagger}_{\rm BK}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}))\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

\displaystyle\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}\iota_{\rm BK}}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h,C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

The third map is a filtered quasi-isomorphism by Lemma
3.20
. It remains to show that so is the second map, i.e., that the map

(3.36)

\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},}

is a filtered strict quasi-isomorphism. Indeed, look at the cohomology of both sides. On the right hand side, arguing as in
[
17
, Sec. 3.2.2]
, we get

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}.

For the left hand side, we compute

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}^{i}(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\widetilde{H}^{i}(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\circ}_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{h}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\circ}_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}.

Remark 3.37
.

Here, for a pair of affinoids
X_{h}\Subset X_{h+1}
as above, we define, slightly abusively,
the (naive) interior
X^{\circ}_{h+1}
as the connected component of
{\rm Int}(X_{h+1})
containing
X_{h}
. See
[
48
, Appendix]
for a discussion of (relative) interiors. By
[
7
, Prop. 2.5.8]
, this definition is functorial in the pair
X_{h}\Subset X_{h+1}
.
Moreover,
X^{\circ}_{h+1}
is Stein and its complement in
X_{h+1}
is open and quasi-compact.

The second and the third isomorphisms above follow from:

(1)

the fact that the cohomology
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\circ}_{h})
is a finite rank vector space over
K
with its canonical topology (by
[
28
, Th. 3.1]
);

(2)

point (1) implies the quasi-isomorphism

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\circ}_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\circ}_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

proved as in
[
17
, Sec. 3.2.2]
;

(3)

the system
\{H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\circ}_{h})\}_{h}
is essentially constant and isomorphic to
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)
;

(4)

point (3) implies that the system
\{{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{\circ}_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\}_{h}
is essentially constant and isomorphic to
{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
.

This proves that the map (
3.36
) is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

We shall need to argue more that it is a filtered strict quasi-isomorphism as well. We argue by induction on
r\geq 0
; the base case of
r=0
being proved above. For the inductive step (
r-1\Rightarrow r
) consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r-1}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{F^{r-1}({\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{F^{r}({\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}C)}

The left and the right vertical triangles are distinguished by (
3.14
) and (
3.30
), respectively. The bottom map is clearly a strict quasi-isomorphism; the top map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by the inductive assumption. It follows that so is the middle horizontal map, as wanted.

We finish the proof of the second claim of our proposition by noting that the map
{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{h}\iota_{\rm BK}
in (
3.35
) is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Lemma
3.20
.

For the third claim of the proposition, it suffices to argue locally for the dagger cohomologies. Hence we can assume that
X\simeq Y_{C}
for a smooth dagger affinoid
Y
defined over
K
.
And then the wanted
t
-completeness follows from the second claim of the proposition and the fact that, since our tensor products are projective and the Mittag-Leffler condition is satisfied, the canonical map

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(Y)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{r}({\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(Y)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r}))

is a filtered strict quasi-isomorphism.
∎

4.
Geometric Hyodo-Kato morphisms

This section is devoted to the definition of compatible rigid analytic (for
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
)
and overconvergent (for
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
) Hyodo-Kato cohomologies
{\rm R}\Gamma_{{\rm HK},\breve{F}}(X)
.
For a general rigid analytic variety, the Hyodo-Kato cohomology is in general quite ugly (not separated
and, locally, infinite dimensional), but for dagger varieties the Hyodo-Kato cohomology
has nice properties (separated and, locally, finite dimensional). On the other hand
(Lemma
4.17
),
if
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
is partially proper, then the rigid analytic
and overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomologies give the same result:
if
X^{\dagger}
is the associated dagger variety,
the natural map
{\rm R}\Gamma_{{\rm HK},\breve{F}}(X^{\dagger})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{{\rm HK},\breve{F}}({X})
is a
strict quasi-isomorphism (Corollary
4.32
).

We define
{\rm R}\Gamma_{{\rm HK},\breve{F}}(X)
for dagger varieties by, locally, going to the limit
over a presentation in the Hyodo-Kato cohomology for rigid analytic varieties, and globalizing.
This definition is much more flexible than Grosse-Klönne’s
[
29
]
, and we show
(Lemma
4.14
) that the two definitions give rise to the same cohomology.

The rigid analytic
and overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomologies are related (Theorem
4.6
and Theorem
4.27
)
to the rigid analytic
and overconvergent de Rham and
{\bf B}_{\operatorname{dR}}^{+}
-cohomologies by
the Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphisms in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})
and
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})
:

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),\quad\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

4.1.
Rigid analytic setting

We start our definitions of Hyodo-Kato morphisms with rigid-analytic varieties.

4.1.1.
Completed Hyodo-Kato cohomology

The completed Hyodo-Kato cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{0}_{1})
that appeared in the proof of Theorem
2.22
has better topological properties than the classical Hyodo-Kato cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{1})
(being over
p
-complete field
\breve{F}
instead of
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
). Because of this we will often use it.

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
. Let
{\mathscr{A}}^{c}_{\operatorname{HK}}

(
c
stands for "completion") be the
\eta
-étale sheafification of the presheaf
{\mathscr{X}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
on
{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{{\rm ss},b}
. We
set in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}^{c}_{\operatorname{HK}}).

It is a dg
\breve{F}
-algebra equipped with a Frobenius, monodromy action, and a continuous action of
{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
, if
X
is defined over
K
. It is equipped with the topology induced from the topology of the
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
’s.

Unwinding the definitions, using the base change quasi-isomorphism (
2.21
), and globalizing we obtain that the canonical morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})

(4.1)

\beta:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. It implies:

Lemma 4.2
.

(Local-global compatibility)

For
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{{\rm ss}}_{C}
, the canonical morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})

(4.3)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

We can pass from
K
to
\breve{K}:=K\breve{F}
(which amounts to passing from
F
to
\breve{F}
for the absolutely unramified subfields) without changing the cohomologies in (
4.3
). And then we can simply use local-global compatibility for
(\breve{F})^{\operatorname{nr}}=\breve{F}
-cohomology (see
[
20
, Prop. 4.23]
).
∎

4.1.2.
Geometric rigid analytic Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphisms

We will now use Theorem
2.22
to define, both local and global, geometric Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphisms.

(i)
Local setting.
We will define two types of Hyodo-Kato morphisms: Hyodo-Kato-to-de Rham and Hyodo-Kato-to-
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
.

Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\rm{ss},b}
.
The Hyodo-Kato-to-de Rham morphism is defined by the composition in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})
:

(4.4)
