\displaystyle\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C\xrightarrow[\sim]{\varepsilon_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{HK}}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1}/\overline{S})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}).

It is a natural strict quasi-isomorphism.

For the Hyodo-Kato-to-
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
morphism we have:

Corollary 4.5
.

Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}_{C}^{\rm{ss},b}
. There exists a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Moreover, we have the commutative diagram in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1\otimes\theta}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C}).}

Proof.

To define
\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}
, we use the natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1}^{0})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

from Lemma
2.31
and compose it with the strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\kappa:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}

from the proof of Lemma
3.24
.

Commutativity of the diagram follows from Lemma
2.31
.
∎

(ii)
Global setting.
We can now state the main theorem of this chapter:

Theorem 4.6
.

(Geometric Hyodo-Kato isomorphisms)

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
. We have the natural Hyodo-Kato strict
quasi-isomorphisms in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})
and
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

(4.7)

\displaystyle\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),\quad\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

that are compatible via the maps
\theta
and
\vartheta
.

Proof.

Globalize the local strict quasi-isomorphisms from Corollary
2.31
and Corollary
4.5
.
∎

(iii)
Complements.
In a similar fashion, the local strict quasi-isomorphism

\varepsilon^{\rm HK}_{\operatorname{st}}
from Theorem
2.22
induces the natural strict
quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

(4.8)

\varepsilon^{\rm HK}_{\operatorname{st}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}},

where we set in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

(4.9)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},1})),

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})({\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},1})),

with the homotopy colimit is taken over
\eta
-étale quasi-compact hypercoverings
{\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
from
{\mathscr{M}}^{\operatorname{ss},b}_{C}
.
Applying the map
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
given by sending
\log(\lambda_{p})\mapsto 0
to the morphism (
4.8
) we obtain
the strict
quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}})

(4.10)

\varepsilon^{\rm HK}_{\operatorname{cr}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X).

4.2.
The overconvergent setting

We are now ready to define the overconvergent geometric Hyodo-Kato morphism. We do it locally by using, via presentations, the rigid-analytic geometric Hyodo-Kato morphism constructed in the previous section and then we glue. The advantage of this approach is that, by construction, the overconvergent and the rigid analytic geometric Hyodo-Kato morphisms are compatible. This is in contrast to
[
17
]
,
[
20
]
, where a lot of effort was devoted to proving compatibility between the overconvergent construction due to Grosse-Klönne, and the rigid-analytic construction due to Hyodo-Kato
33
33

33

Recently, Ertl-Yamada in
[
23
]
have introduced a particularly simple definition of overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology for weak-formal semistable schemes and equally simple definition of the relevant Hyodo-Kato map. Their construction is compatible with the crystalline Hyodo-Kato analog when the scheme is proper. It is likely that their construction can be extended to the set-up needed in this paper.
.

4.2.1.
Overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology via presentations of dagger structures

In this section we introduce a definition of overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology using presentations of dagger structures (see
[
48
, Appendix]
,
[
20
, Sec. 6.3]
). We show that the so defined Hyodo-Kato cohomology, a priori different from the one defined by Grosse-Klönne, is, in fact, strictly quasi-isomorphic to it.

(i)
Local definition
. Let
X
be a dagger affinoid over
L=K,C
. Let
{\rm pres}(X):=\{X_{h}\}_{h\in{\bf N}}
be a presentation of dagger structures.
Define in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})
:

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X):=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})

and equip it with the induced Frobenius and monodromy.
We have a natural map

(4.11)

\alpha^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)

defined as the composition

(4.12)

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{\circ}_{h})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{{\circ},\dagger}_{h})\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X).

The third map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Corollary
3.6
: this is because the
interior
X^{\circ}_{h}
is Stein. Note that the proof of the cited corollary relies on a nontrivial comparison result between the rigid analytic and Grosse-Klönne’s overconvergent Hyodo-Kato morphisms.
The map
\alpha^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}
is functorial (see Remark
3.37
).

(ii)
Globalization
. For a general smooth dagger variety
X
over
L
, using the natural equivalence of analytic topoi

(4.13)

{\rm Sh}({\rm SmAff}^{\dagger}_{L,\operatorname{\acute{e}t}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm Sh}({\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{L,\operatorname{\acute{e}t}})\

we define the sheaf
{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\dagger}
on
X_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}
as the sheaf associated to the presheaf defined by
U\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\dagger}(U),U\in{\rm SmAff}^{\dagger}_{L}
,
U\to X
an étale map. We define in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{F_{L}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}).

If
L=K
, it is a dg
F
-algebra. If
L=C
, it is a dg
{F}^{\operatorname{nr}}
-algebra equipped with a Frobenius, monodromy action, and a continuous action of
{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
if
X
is defined over
K
. Its topology is induced from the topology of the
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)
’s.

Globalizing the map
\alpha_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\dagger}
from (
4.11
) we obtain a natural
map in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{F_{L}})

\alpha_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X).

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
L=K,C
.

(1)

The above map
\alpha_{\operatorname{HK}}
is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

(Local-global compatibility)
If
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid the natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{F_{L}})

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

For the first claim, by étale descent,
we may assume that
X
comes from a smooth dagger affinoid. Looking at the composition (
4.12
) defining the map
\alpha^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}
we see that it suffices to show that the natural map

(4.15)

\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\rm GK}(X^{{\circ},\dagger}_{h})\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. But this was shown in the proof of Proposition 6.17 in
[
20
]
. We note that that proof uses the Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphism of Grosse-Klönne to pass to the de Rham cohomology where the analog of (
4.15
) is obvious.

For the second claim, consider the commutative local-global diagram in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{F_{L}})

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)}

The slanted arrow is a strict quasi-isomorphism by the first claim of the lemma. It suffices to show that the left vertical arrow is a strict quasi-isomorphism as well. For that, it suffices to show that the map

\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\rm GK}(X^{{\circ},\dagger}_{h})\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)

appearing in the definition (
4.12
) of the map
\alpha^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}
is a strict quasi-isomorphism but this was just shown above.
∎

(iii)
Completed overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology.

We can define the
completed overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology
by a similar procedure to the one used above. It will have better topological properties than its classical version. Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
.
Let
{\rm pres}(X)=\{X_{h}\}_{h\in{\bf N}}
. Define in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X):=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h}).

For a general smooth dagger variety over
C
, we can globalize the above definition and obtain the
sheaf
{\mathscr{A}}^{c}_{\operatorname{HK}}
for the
\eta
-étale topology on
{\mathscr{M}}^{\dagger,{\rm ss}}_{C}
and
cohomology

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}^{c}_{\operatorname{HK}})\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}}).

It is a dg
\breve{F}
-algebra equipped with a Frobenius, monodromy action, and a continuous action of
{\mathscr{G}}_{K}
, if
X
is defined over
K
. It is equipped with the topology induced from the topology of the
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)
’s.

We have the local-global compatibility by Lemma
4.14

(replace, without loss of information,
F
by
\breve{F}
).

(iv)
Completed overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology ala Grosse-Klönne.
But we can also define the completed overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology as in the rigid analytic case, by modifying the definition of the overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology of Grosse-Klönne. That is, we can
set
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}^{\rm GK}({\mathscr{X}}_{1}):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}_{0})
,
for
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{\dagger,{\rm ss}}_{C}
, where
{\mathscr{X}}_{0}:={\mathscr{X}}_{\overline{k}}
, and globalize. We will denote by
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}^{\rm GK}(X),X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
, the so obtained cohomology in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})
.

We easily check that we have strict quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})
:

(4.16)

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}^{\rm GK}({\mathscr{X}}_{1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\rm GK}({\mathscr{X}}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F},\quad{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{\dagger,{\rm ss}},

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}^{\rm GK}(X)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\rm GK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F},\quad X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}^{\dagger}.

We also have local-global compatibility: pass from
F
to
\breve{F}
as in the proof of Lemma
4.2
. This reduces the problem to the local-global compatibility for the usual Hyodo-Kato cohomology
of Grosse-Klönne and this we know is true.

The two definitions of completed overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology give the same objects:

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}^{\dagger}
. There exists a natural strict
quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})

\alpha_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}^{\rm GK}(X).

Proof.

Pass from
F
to
\breve{F}
and use Lemma
4.14
.
∎

(v)
Tensor products.
The following lemma will allow us to pass between tensor products involving the two definitions of overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
W
be a Banach space
34
34

34

In applications,
W
will be most often a period rings.
over
\breve{F}
.

(1)

(Local-global compatibility)
Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
. The canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\breve{F}})

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
. There exists a following commutative diagram in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\breve{F}})

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(W)}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}^{\rm GK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{\operatorname{HK}}(W)}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\rm GK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

Remark 4.19
.

(1) The tensor product
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W
is
defined in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\breve{F}})
as

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W),

where
\{X_{h}\}
is the presentation of
X
.

(2)
Warning
: One has to be careful with tensor products as in (1) (because we chose projective tensor products hence we lost the commutation with general inductive limits). For example, when
F^{\operatorname{nr}}=\breve{F}
, the tensor product

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W
is already defined. Luckily, in this case, the two definitions give the same tensor product. To see this, note that we have

{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W=(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h}))\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W
. Hence the canonical map

\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W

induces a map
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W.
In the proof of Lemma
4.18
below we will show that this is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

(3) For any smooth dagger variety
X
, the tensor product
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W
is defined by globalizing the tensor product from (1).

Proof.

For (1), we start with the case
W=\breve{F}
. Consider the commutative diagram

(4.20)

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X).}

The bottom map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Lemma
4.14
(replace
F
by
\breve{F}
). The left vertical map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by definition and (
4.1
); the right vertical map is the globalization of the left vertical map hence a strict quasi-isomorphism as well. It follows that the top map is also a strict quasi-isomorphism, as wanted.

Now, for a general
W
, we take the top map in the diagram (
4.20
) and tensor it with
W
over
\breve{F}
to obtain the strict quasi-isomorphism in the top of the commutative diagram

\textstyle{({\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

It remains to show that the left vertical map in the diagram is a strict quasi-isomorphism because then so is the right vertical map (being the globalization of the left vertical map) and then the bottom map as well, as wanted.

Remark 4.21
.

The tensor product in the top row is the usual projective tensor product. Hence the vertical maps are not identities and the statement that they are strict quasi-isomorphisms is not trivial even for
\breve{F}
.

It is clear that the left vertical map is a strict quasi-isomorphism if we drop the dagger and replace
X
with
X_{h}
for the presentation
\{X_{h}\}
of
X
.
It suffices thus to show that the map

(4.22)

\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W)\to(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\breve{F})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. Applying the Hyodo-Kato morphism we pass to the canonical map

\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W\to(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}))\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}W,

which is a strict quasi-isomorphism by
[
17
, 2.1.2]
. Now we go back to the map (
4.22
) by a projection
C\to\breve{F}
.

We pass now to the second claim of the lemma. Assume first that
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid. Then we define the map

\alpha_{\operatorname{HK}}(W):\quad{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\rm GK}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W

as the composition

(4.23)

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W

\displaystyle=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{\circ}_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{{\circ},\dagger}_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W.

The third map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Corollary
3.6
: this is because the interior
X^{\rm o}_{h}
is partially proper.

For a general
X
, we obtain the map
\alpha_{\operatorname{HK}}(W)
by globalizing the above definition. Changing
F
into
\breve{F}
in the definition of
\alpha_{\operatorname{HK}}(W)
, we get the map
\alpha_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(W)
compatible with the map
\alpha_{\operatorname{HK}}(W)
. This gives us the commutative diagram we wanted. Moreover, it is clear from the definitions that the right vertical map in the diagram is a strict quasi-isomorphism. The top map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Lemma
4.17
.
The left vertical map is a strict quasi-isomorphism because we can check it locally where claim (1) reduces us to the dagger cohomology of an affinoid and there this is clear from the definitions. It follows then that the bottom map is a strict quasi-isomorphism as well, as wanted.
∎

(vi)
Properties of overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology.
Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. Recall that (see
[
20
, Prop. 4.38]
) the Hyodo-Kato cohomology
\widetilde{H}^{*}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)
is classical.
If
X
is quasi-compact it is a finite dimensional
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
-vector space with its natural topology. For a general
X
,
it is a limit in
C_{F}
of finite dimensional
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
-vector spaces.
The endomorphism
\varphi
on
H^{*}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)
is a homeomorphism.

We will need the following computation later on:

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. Let
W
be a Banach space with an
\breve{F}
-module structure.

(1)

If
X
is quasi-compact then the cohomology of the complex
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}W
is classical and we have an
\breve{F}
-linear topological isomorphism

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W)\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W,\quad i\geq 0.

(2)

Take an increasing admissible covering
\{U_{n}\}_{n\in{\bf N}}
of
X
by quasi-compact dagger varieties
U_{n}
. Then we have a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\breve{F}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{n}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n,C})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W).

The cohomology of
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W
is classical and we have, for
i\geq 0
, an
\breve{F}
-linear topological isomorphism

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W)\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W:=\operatorname{lim}_{n}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n}){\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W).

In particular, it is a Fréchet space
35
35

35

We note that
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n})
is a finite rank vector space over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
equipped with the canonical topology.
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.18
, we may replace
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(-)
with Grosse-Klönne’s version
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}^{\rm GK}(-)
. Let
X
be quasi-compact. Consider an étale hypercovering
{\mathscr{U}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
of
X
built from quasi-compact models from
{\mathscr{M}}^{\dagger,\operatorname{ss},b}_{C}
. By
[
17
, Ex. 3.16]
, claim (1) is true for every
{\mathscr{U}}_{i,C}
. Hence we have the spectral sequence

E_{2}^{i,j}=H^{{\rm GK},i}_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{U}}_{j,C}){\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\Rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i+j}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}W).

The terms of the spectral sequence are Banach spaces and the differentials in the spectral sequence are
W
-linear. Since the Hyodo-Kato cohomology groups

H^{{\rm GK},i}_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{U}}_{j,C})
are of finite rank, claim (1) follows.

Having (1), claim (2) follows just as in the proof of
[
17
, 3.26]
(note that the system
\{H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n}){\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}W\}_{n\in{\bf N}}
satisfies the Mittag-Leffler condition).
∎

4.2.2.
Overconvergent geometric Hyodo-Kato morphism via presentations of dagger structures

In this section we introduce a definition of overconvergent geometric Hyodo-Kato morphism using presentations of dagger structures.

(i)
Local definition
. Let
X
be a dagger affinoid over
C
. Let
{\rm pres}(X)=\{X_{h}\}
.

Define natural Hyodo-Kato morphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\breve{F}})

(4.25)

\displaystyle\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),\quad\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

as the compositions

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}(\iota_{\operatorname{HK}})}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})={\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}(\iota_{\operatorname{HK}})}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})={\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

They are compatible via the map
\theta:{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to C
.

Proposition 4.26
.

The linearizations of the Hyodo-Kato morphisms in (
4.25
) yield compatible natural strict Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphisms in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\breve{F}})
and
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\displaystyle\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),\quad\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Proof.

For the first map, we need to show that the map

(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h}))\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}C\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}(\iota_{\operatorname{HK}})}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. But this map fits into a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}(\iota_{\operatorname{HK}})}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h}))\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}(\iota_{\operatorname{HK}})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h})}

The bottom term is just the overconvergent de Rham cohomology and its cohomology is classical and a finite rank vector space over
C
with its canonical topology. Via the vertical strict quasi-isomorphism the same is true of the upper left term. The upper right term is strictly quasi-isomorphic to
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C
, which, by Lemma
4.18
and Proposition
4.24
, also has classical cohomology that is finite rank over
C
. Hence, looking at the above diagram one cohomology degree at a time, we obtain a commutative diagram of finite rank vector spaces over
C
. These ranks are, in fact, equal: this is clear for the bottom and the upper left term; for the upper right term consider the maps:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{F}^{\operatorname{nr}}}C\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK}}}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X).

The first map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Lemma
4.18
. The second map is the Grosse-Klönne Hyodo-Kato morphism and it is a strict quasi-isomorphism by
[
20
, 5.15]
. Hence the rank in question is the same as that of the corresponding de Rham cohomology, as wanted.

For the second map in our proposition, we argue in a similar fashion. We need to show that the map

(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h}))\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}(\iota_{\operatorname{HK}})}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. But this map fits into a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}(\iota_{\operatorname{HK}})}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X_{h}))\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}(\iota_{\operatorname{HK}})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}

The vertical map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by (
4.7
). The horizontal map can be shown to be a strict quasi-isomorphism by an argument analogous to the one used in the proof of Proposition
3.29
.
It follows that so is the slanted map, as wanted.
∎

(ii)
Globalization
. For a general smooth dagger variety
X
over
C
,
globalizing the maps
\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}
from (
4.25
), we obtain compatible
natural maps in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\breve{F}})

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),\quad\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Theorem 4.27
.

(Overconvergent Hyodo-Kato isomorphisms)

The linearizations of the above Hyodo-Kato morphisms yields compatible natural strict quasi-isomorphisms in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{C})
and
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\displaystyle\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),

\displaystyle\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

Proof.

Looking at
\eta
-étale hypercoverings and using that our tensor products commute with products, we may assume
X
to be a dagger affinoid and then the result is known by Proposition
4.26
.
∎

(iii)
Application.
As an immediate application of the overconvergent Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphisms we get the local-global compatibility for
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology: .

Corollary 4.28
.

(Local-global compatibility)
Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
. The canonical morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

(4.29)

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}

The vertical arrows are strict quasi-isomorphisms by Proposition
4.26
and Theorem
4.27
. The top arrow is a strict quasi-isomorphism by the local-global compatibility for completed overconvergent Hyodo-Kato cohomology.
It follows that so is the bottom horizontal arrow, proving that the map (
4.29
) is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

To show that this map is a filtered strict quasi-isomorphism, we will argue by induction on
r\geq 0
. The inductive step uses
the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X),}

in which the rows are distinguished triangles by Proposition
3.29
and its proof. The first and the third vertical maps are strict quasi-isomorphism by the inductive hypothesis and by the local-global property for filtered de Rham cohomology (see
[
20
, Sec. 5.1]
), respectively. It follows that the middle vertical map is a strict quasi-isomorphism as well, as wanted.
∎

4.2.3.
Comparison with the rigid analytic constructions

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
L=K,C
. Let
\widehat{X}
be its completion.

Lemma 4.30
.

(1)

There is a natural morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{F^{\rm nr}})

(4.31)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X}).

(2)

Let
L=C
. There are compatible natural morphisms in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})
and
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(\widehat{X}),\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

They are compatible with the map (
4.31
).

(3)

The morphism in (2) are compatible with the Hyodo-Kato morphisms, i.e., we have the commutative diagrams in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\breve{F}})

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(\widehat{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}),}

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(\widehat{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}

Proof.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger affinoid over
L
with the presentation
\{X_{h}\}
. Using the compatible maps
\widehat{X}\to X_{h}
, we define the map

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\to\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X}).

It globalizes to give the map in (
4.31
).

We proceed in a similar way for the other two cohomologies. The stated compatibilities follow easily from the definitions.
∎

The Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphisms imply the following:

Corollary 4.32
.

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
.
If
X
is partially proper, then the canonical morphisms in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\breve{F}})
and
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

(4.33)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(\widehat{X}),\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

are strict quasi-isomorphisms.

Proof.

For the first map, consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X})}

It implies that the top arrow is a strict quasi-isomorphism. Splitting off
\breve{F}
from
C
we obtain
the claim of the corollary.

For the second map, in the unfiltered case, consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})}

The top arrow is a strict quasi-isomorphism by what was just proved. It implies that the bottom arrow is a strict quasi-isomorphism, as wanted.

To treat filtrations, we proceed by induction on the filtration level
r
(the base case of
r=0
just proved). The inductive step (
r-1\Rightarrow r
) uses the commutative diagram

\textstyle{F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\textstyle{F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}),}

in which the rows are distinguished triangles by Proposition
3.29
and Proposition
3.13
. The first vertical map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by the inductive hypothesis.
It follows that the middle vertical map is a strict quasi-isomorphism as well, as wanted.
∎

5.
Overconvergent geometric syntomic cohomology

In this section we will define overconvergent geometric syntomic cohomology and prove a comparison theorem for smooth dagger affinoids and Stein varieties over
C
.

5.1.
Local-global compatibility for rigid analytic geometric syntomic cohomology

Recall that in
[
20
, Sec. 4.1]
the syntomic cohomology

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\in{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
of a rigid analytic variety
X
is defined by
\eta
-étale descent from the crystalline syntomic cohomology of Fontaine-Messing.
The latter is defined as the fiber (
{\mathscr{X}}
is a semistable formal scheme over
{\mathscr{O}}_{C}
equipped with its canonical
log-structure)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}({\mathscr{X}},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=[F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varphi-p^{r}}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})],

where the (logarithmic) crystalline cohomology is absolute (i.e., over
{\bf Z}_{p}
).
By definition, it fits into the distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

(5.1)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(1))\to[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)]^{\varphi=p^{r}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)/F^{r}

We were not able to prove the local-global compatibility for this syntomic cohomology in
[
20
]
: the usual technique is to pass from the second term of (
5.1
) to Hyodo-Kato cohomology and from the third term – to filtered de Rham cohomology; then one passes, via the Hyodo-Kato quasi-isomorphism, from Hyodo-Kato cohomology to de Rham cohomology and we do have local-global compatibility for filtered de Rham cohomology. The problem was: we did not have then the Hyodo-Kato morphism. But we have it now
thanks to Theorem
4.6
, so in this section we
will prove the local-global compatibility for rigid analytic geometric syntomic cohomology that we will need.

We start with stating such a compatibility for absolute crystalline cohomology.

Lemma 5.2
.

(Crystalline local-global compatibility)
Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{{\rm ss},b}_{C}
.
The canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C})

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

We have the commutative diagram in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}})

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C})}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}({\mathscr{X}}^{0}_{1})_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\operatorname{cr}}^{\operatorname{HK}}}
\scriptstyle{\beta\otimes\operatorname{Id}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{cr}}}

The bottom map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Lemma
4.2
. Hence so is the top map, as wanted.
∎

Proposition 5.3
.

(Syntomic local-global compatibility)

Let
{\mathscr{X}}\in{\mathscr{M}}^{{\rm ss},b}_{C}
. Let
r\geq 0
.
The canonical map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}({\mathscr{X}},{\bf Z}_{p}(r))_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}({\mathscr{X}}_{C},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. Here
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}({\mathscr{X}},{\bf Z}_{p}(r))
is the syntomic cohomology of Fontaine-Messing
[
25
]

(see also
[
4
]
)
.

Proof.

Set
X:={\mathscr{X}}_{C}
.
First, we define a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
:

\displaystyle\iota_{2}:\quad

\displaystyle\big[[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}({X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\big]

\displaystyle\to[[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({X})]^{\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\operatorname{can}}}\,{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({X})/F^{r}]={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}({X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

where we set

[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}:=\left[\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 123.1148pt\raise-24.23112pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{N}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 123.1148pt\raise-39.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.69019pt\raise-42.52614pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.57501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{1-\varphi_{r-1}}$}}}\kern
