81.5574pt\raise-48.46225pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\right]

For that, it suffices to define the maps
\iota_{\rm BK}^{1}
and
\iota_{\rm BK}^{2}
in the following diagram, with the first map being Frobenius equivariant, and to show that this diagram commutes in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
:

(5.4)

\textstyle{[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm BK}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm BK}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({X})/F^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\kappa}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

Here the map
\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}
is the one from (
4.8
). It is Frobenius equivariant. We set:
\iota_{\rm BK}^{1}:=\beta^{-1}\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}
and
\iota_{\rm BK}^{2}:=\kappa^{-1}
. Since the map
\beta
is Frobenius equivariant so is the map
\iota_{\rm BK}^{1}
. By definition, all the pieces of the diagram commute and the maps
\iota_{\rm BK}^{1},\iota_{\rm BK}^{2}
are strict quasi-isomorphisms.

The morphism
\iota_{2}
has a compatible local version. Now, the wanted local-global compatibility, via the strict quasi-isomorphisms
\iota_{2}
, follows from local-global compatibility for Hyodo-Kato cohomology and filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology, proved in Proposition
3.1
and Lemma
3.23
, respectively.
∎

The proof of Proposition
5.3
actually shows the following:

Corollary 5.5
.

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
and
r\geq 0
. There exist a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

(5.6)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq\big[[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\big].

We like to call the expression on the right
the Bloch-Kato syntomic cohomology
because it resembles the definition of Bloch-Kato Selmer groups in
[
12
]
.

5.2.
Twisted Hyodo-Kato cohomology

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. In this section we will study the twisted Hyodo-Kato
cohomology in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

(5.7)

{\rm HK}(X,r):=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},\quad r\geq 0,

where
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)
is the geometric Hyodo-Kato cohomology defined in
[
20
, Sec. 4.3.1]
and
we set in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{F}^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})(U_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})),

where the homotopy colimit is taken over étale affinoid hypercoverings
U_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
from
{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
.
We wrote
[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}
for the homotopy limit of the commutative diagram in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi-p^{r}}
\scriptstyle{N}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{N}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p\varphi-p^{r}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}.}

The following proposition generalizes the computations from
[
17
, Sec. 3.2.2]
done in the case when
X
has a semistable integral model over a finite extension of
K
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. Let
r\geq 0
.

(1)

If
X
is quasi-compact then the cohomology of the complex
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
is classical and we have an isomorphism of
(\varphi,N)
-modules over
F^{\rm nr}

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}},\quad i\geq 0.

(2)

If
X
is quasi-compact there is a natural isomorphism

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm HK}(X,r))\simeq(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},\quad i\geq 0,

of Banach spaces. In particular,
\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm HK}(X,r))
is classical.

(3)

Take an increasing admissible covering
\{U_{n}\}_{n\in{\bf N}}
of
X
by quasi-compact dagger varieties
U_{n}
. Then we have a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in

{\mathscr{D}}(C_{\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{n}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n,C})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}).

The cohomology of
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
is classical and we have, for
i\geq 0
, an isomorphism of
(\varphi,N)
-modules over
\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:=\operatorname{lim}_{n}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n}){\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}).

In particular, it is a Fréchet space
36
36

36

We note that
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n})
is a finite rank vector space over
F^{\operatorname{nr}}
equipped with the canonical topology.
.

(4)

The cohomology
\widetilde{H}^{i}([{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}})
,
i\geq 0
, is classical and we have natural isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

{H}^{i}([{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}})\simeq(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},\quad i\geq 0.

In particular, the space
{H}^{i}([{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}})
is Fréchet. Moreover,

{H}^{i}([{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0})\simeq(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0}\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}},

where the last isomorphism is not, in general, Galois equivariant (in the case
X
comes from
X_{K}
over
K
).

Proof.

Since
\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}
is a Banach space over
\breve{F}
, claims (1) and (3) are a special case of Proposition
4.24
.
Claim (2) follows from (1) just as in the proof of
[
17
, Lemma 3.20]
. Finally, claim (4) follows from (3) is proved as in
[
17
, Lemma 3.28]
.
∎

5.2.1.
A variant of the twisted Hyodo-Kato cohomology

There is a variant of the twisted Hyodo-Kato
cohomology in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\widetilde{\rm HK}(X,r):=[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}},\quad r\geq 0,

that we will often use.
Here we set in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{F}^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}(({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}},\iota}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})(U_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})),

where the homotopy colimit is taken over étale affinoid hypercoverings
U_{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
from
{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
. We have in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\simeq\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}},\iota}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}),

where
\{X_{h}\}
is the presentation of
X
. It is easy to check that this tensor product satisfies local-global compatibility.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
. The canonical morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{F^{\rm nr}})

\widetilde{\rm HK}(X,r)\to{\rm HK}(X,r),\quad r\geq 0.

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the canonical morphism

[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\to[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. For that, from the definitions of both sides, we can assume that
X
is a dagger affinoid. Then this map can be rewritten as

[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}},\iota}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\to[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0},

which, by Lemma
4.18
, can be written as

\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}([{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}},\iota}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0})\to\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}([{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widetilde{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}),

for the presentation
\{X_{h}\}
of
X
. But this map is a strict quasi-isomorphism because so is the canonical map

[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}},\iota}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\to[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widetilde{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0},

by the same argument as the one used to show (
2.29
).
∎

5.3.
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. In this section we will study the filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
and its
quotients

{\rm DR}(X,r):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r},\quad r\geq 0.

We note that, immediately from the distinguished triangle (
3.30
), we obtain

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
.
Let
r\geq 0
. We have a distinguished triangle in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm DR}(X,r-1)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,{\rm DR}(X,r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)/F^{r}

By Theorem
4.27
, we have the strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

(5.11)

\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

It yields the following computation:

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
.

(1)

If
X
is quasi-compact then the cohomology of the complex
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is classical and we have

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},\quad i\geq 0.

(2)

Take an increasing admissible covering
\{U_{n}\}_{n\in{\bf N}}
of
X
by quasi-compact dagger varieties
U_{n}
. Then we have a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{n}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(U_{n}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

The cohomology of
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is classical and we have, for
i\geq 0
,

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{n}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(U_{n}){\otimes}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

In particular, it is a Fréchet space
37
37

37

Recall that
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(U_{n})
is a finite rank vector space over
\breve{F}
equipped with the canonical topology.
.

Proof.

Using the Hyodo-Kato morphism (
5.11
), we may pass to the computation of the cohomology of the complex
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
.
Since
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\simeq\prod_{k\geq 0}Ct^{k}
in
C_{\breve{F}}
, we have

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\simeq\prod_{k\geq 0}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}Ct^{k})

and we can use Lemma
4.18
to pass to
{\rm R}\Gamma^{\rm GK}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
. Then the proof of Proposition
4.24
goes through.
∎

5.3.1.
Varieties over
K

Before studying filtrations on
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology we will look more carefully at the example of varieties defined over
K
.

Recall that (see
[
20
, Sec. 5.1]
), for a smooth dagger variety
X
over
L
,
L=K,C
, the de Rham cohomology
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)
is classical.
If
X
is quasi-compact it is a finite dimensional
L
-vector space with its natural topology. For a general
X
,
it is a limit in
C_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
of finite dimensional
L
-vector spaces (hence a Fréchet space).

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{K}
. By Proposition
3.29
, we have the strict quasi-isomorphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\displaystyle\iota_{\rm BK}:\quad{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{C}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}),

\displaystyle{\rm DR}(X_{C},r)

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}.

(i)
Example: Stein varieties over
K
.

Assume that
X
is Stein. We easily see that in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{K})

(5.13)

\displaystyle F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\simeq F^{r}(\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle=({\mathscr{O}}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}F^{r}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\Omega^{1}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}F^{r-1}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\to\cdots)

\displaystyle{\rm DR}(X_{C},r)=({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)

\displaystyle\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\simeq(\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}

\displaystyle=({\mathscr{O}}({X})

\displaystyle\widehat{\otimes}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r})\to\Omega^{1}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{r-1})\to\cdots\to\Omega^{r-1}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{1})).

In low degrees we have

\displaystyle{\rm DR}(X_{C},0)

\displaystyle=0,\quad{\rm DR}(X_{C},1)\simeq{\mathscr{O}}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C,

\displaystyle{\rm DR}(X_{C},2)

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathscr{O}}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}({\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}/F^{2})\to\Omega^{1}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}C).

Recall that, because
X
is Stein, the de Rham complex is built from Fréchet spaces and it has strict differentials. Arguing just as in
[
17
, Ex. 3.30]
it follows that:

(1)

the complexes
F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
and
{\rm DR}(X_{C},r)
are built from Fréchet spaces;

(2)

their differentials are strict;

(3)

and
the cohomologies
\widetilde{H}^{i}F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
and
\widetilde{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(X_{C},r)
are classical and Fréchet.

(ii)
Example: Affinoids over
K
.
Assume now that
X
is an affinoid. Then the computation is a bit more complicated because the spaces
\Omega^{i}(X)
and
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
(an
LB
-space and a Fréchet space, respectively) do not work together well with tensor products. However, if we use the fact that
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\simeq\prod_{k\geq 0}Ct^{k}
in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{K})
, we get the strict quasi-isomorphisms

\Omega^{i}({X})\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\Omega^{i}({X})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},

which implies the strict quasi-isomorphisms
from (
5.13
).

Then, arguing just as in
[
17
, Ex. 3.30]
,
one shows that the cohomology
\widetilde{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(X_{C},r)
is classical and that it is an
{LB}
-space. Also, we easily see that the differentials in the complex

F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
are strict; hence
the cohomology
\widetilde{H}^{i}F^{r}({\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is classical.

(iii)
General varieties over
K
.
The following computation can be done in the same way as the computation in Proposition
5.12
.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
K
.

(1)

If
X
is quasi-compact then the cohomology of the complex
{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is classical and we have

(5.15)

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{K}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},\quad i\geq 0.

(2)

Take an increasing admissible covering
\{U_{n}\}_{n\in{\bf N}}
of
X
by quasi-compact dagger varieties
U_{n}
. Then we have a natural strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{n}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(U_{n})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

The cohomology of
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
is classical and we have, for
i\geq 0
,

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{n}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(U_{n}){\otimes}_{K}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

In particular, it is a Fréchet space
38
38

38

We note that
H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(U_{n})
is a finite rank vector space over
K
equipped with the canonical topology.
.

5.3.2.
Stein varieties and affinoid over
C

If
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
then it is defined over a finite field extension of
K
and its de Rham type cohomologies have properties listed in Section
5.3.1
.

In the case of Stein varieties we need to argue a bit more.

Proposition 5.16
.

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
be Stein and
r\geq 0
.
Then

(1)

concerning the complex
{\rm DR}(X,r)
, we have:

(a)

the cohomology
\widetilde{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(X,r)
is classical and Fréchet.

(b)

we have a strictly exact sequence

0\to\Omega^{i}(X)/\operatorname{Im}d\to{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(X,r)\to H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{r-i-1}\to 0

(2)

the cohomology
\widetilde{H}^{i}F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is classical and Fréchet.

Proof.

Concerning claim (1), cover
X
with a Stein covering by affinoids
\{U_{n}\}
,
n\in{\bf N}
. Since every affinoid
U_{n}
is defined over a finite extension of
K
, we have the strict exact sequences from
[
17
, Ex. 3.30]

0\to\Omega^{i}(U_{n})/\operatorname{Im}d\to{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(U_{n},r)\to H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(U_{n}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{r-i-1}\to 0

All the terms are classical and Hausdorff.
We claim that, taking their
\lim_{n}
, we obtain

(5.17)

\displaystyle 0\to\lim_{n}(\Omega^{i}(U_{n})/\operatorname{Im}d)\to\lim_{n}{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(U_{n},r)\to\lim_{n}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(U_{n}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{r-i-1})\to 0

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(U_{n},r)\simeq{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(U_{n}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{r-i-1}=0

Indeed, the sequence is strictly exact since
{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}\Omega^{i}(U_{n})=0
. For the same reason we have the isomorphism between
{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim
’s. Since we have Hyodo-Kato isomorphisms

H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(U_{n}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(U_{n})\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
and the Hyodo-Kato cohomology
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(U_{n})
is of finite rank, these
{\mathrm{R}}^{1}\lim_{n}
vanish. From (
5.17
) we obtain the strictly exact sequence

0\to\Omega^{i}(X)/\operatorname{Im}d\to\widetilde{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(X,r)\to H^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/t^{r-i-1}\to 0

Hence,
\widetilde{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(X,r)
is classical (as an extension of two classical objects). It is also an extension of two Fréchet spaces; which implies that it is, in particular, Hausdorff. It is also a quotient of two Fréchet spaces by construction,
which implies that it is a Fréchet space itself, as wanted.

For claim (2), since we have the Hyodo-Kato strict quasi-isomorphism (from Theorem
4.27
)

\iota_{\rm HK}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

and the cohomology

\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\simeq H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{\breve{F}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}

is classical, we get that the cohomology
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
is also classical and Fréchet.
For
i>r
, we have an isomorphism
\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
(take an exhaustive affinoid covering and use the fact that affinoids are defined over a finite extension of
K
); hence this cohomology is also classical and Fréchet.

For
i\leq r
, we argue by induction on
r
, the base case of
r=0
being shown above. For the inductive step (
r-1\Rightarrow r
), take
the distinguished triangle (
3.30
) and consider the induced long exact sequence

0\to{H}^{i}(F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\partial}}\,{H}^{i+1}(F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))

The injection on the left follows from the fact that
{H}^{i-1}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X))=0
; the terms involving
F^{r-1}
filtration are classical by the inductive hypothesis.

\bullet
If
i<r
, then this yields an isomorphism

{H}^{i}(F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\xrightarrow[\sim]{t}\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})),

showing that
\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))
is classical and Fréchet.

\bullet
For
i=r
, we get a short exact sequence

0\to{H}^{i}(F^{r-1}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{t}}\,\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\vartheta}}\,\ker{\partial}\to 0

Hence,
\widetilde{H}^{i}(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}))
is classical and a Fréchet space by the argument we have used in the case of
\widetilde{H}^{i}{\rm DR}(X,r)
in the proof of claim (1).
∎

5.4.
Overconvergent geometric syntomic cohomology

We are now ready to define overconvergent geometric syntomic cohomology and prove a comparison theorem for smooth dagger affinoids and Stein varieties.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
.
Take
r\geq 0
.
We define the
geometric syntomic cohomology
of
X
as the following mapping fiber
(taken in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
)

(5.18)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle:=[[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}]

\displaystyle=[{\rm HK}(X,r)\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\rm DR}(X,r)].

This is a generalization of the geometric syntomic cohomology introduced in
[
17
, Sec. 3.2.2]
in the case
X
comes from a semistable model over
{\mathscr{O}}_{K}
. We will define below in Section
6.2.1
overconvergent geometric syntomic cohomology via presentations of dagger structures from rigid-analytic geometric syntomic cohomology and show in Proposition
6.6
that the two definitions give strictly quasi-isomorphic cohomologies.

The following proposition generalizes
[
17
, Prop. 3.36]
.

Remark 5.19
.

We will often use an equivalent definition of overconvergent geometric syntomic cohomology:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=[[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}^{{\mathrm{R}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}].

See Lemma
5.9
for why the two definitions give the same object (up to a canonical strict quasi-isomorphism).

Proposition 5.20
.

Let

.

. There is a natural map of strictly exact sequences

Proof.

6.
Two comparison morphisms

In this section we define two comparison morphisms: from geometric syntomic cohomology of a smooth dagger variety to geometric syntomic cohomology of its completion and between geometric syntomic cohomology of a smooth dagger variety and its pro-étale cohomology.
We also prove that the first morphism is a quasi-isomorphism
for partially proper varieties (Theorem
6.2
)
and the second morphism is a quasi-isomorphism in a stable range (Theorem
6.9
).

6.1.
From overconvergent to rigid analytic geometric syntomic cohomology

We start with a morphism from geometric syntomic cohomology of a smooth dagger variety to geometric syntomic cohomology of its completion.

6.1.1.
Construction of the comparison morphism.

Let
X
be a smooth dagger variety over
C
. We will construct a natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

(6.1)

\iota:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

from the syntomic cohomology of
X
to the syntomic cohomology of its completion
\widehat{X}
.

(i)
The map
\iota_{1}
.

First, we note that we have a canonical natural morphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\displaystyle\iota_{1}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle=\big[[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\big]

\displaystyle\rightarrow\big[[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\big]

\displaystyle\xleftarrow{\sim}\big[[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\big].

Indeed, for that it suffices to show that the canonical map

[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\to[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. We may assume that
X
has a semistable weak formal model
{\mathscr{X}}
defined over
{\mathscr{O}}_{K^{\prime}}
. Then the above map is equal to the map

[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}^{0}_{1})\widehat{\otimes}_{K^{\prime},\iota}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\to[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}({\mathscr{X}}^{1}_{0})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{K^{\prime}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}.

But this is a special case of the strict quasi-isomorphism in (
2.29
).

(ii)
The map
\iota_{2}
.

Next, we use the strict quasi-isomorphism in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\displaystyle\iota_{2}:\quad

\displaystyle\big[[{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})_{F}\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\big]

\displaystyle\to[[{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(\widehat{X})]^{\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\operatorname{can}}}\,{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(\widehat{X})/F^{r}]={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

from the proof of Proposition
5.3
.

(iii) Finally, we define the map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\iota:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}({X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

as
\iota:=\iota_{2}\iota_{1}
.

6.1.2.
A comparison theorem

We are now ready to prove our comparison theorem:

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
X
be a partially proper smooth dagger variety over
C
.
The map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\iota:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

We have
\iota=\iota_{2}\iota_{1}
by definition and as we have seen the map
\iota_{2}
is a strict quasi-isomorphism. Hence it remains to show that so is the map
\iota_{1}
. For that, it suffices to show that the following canonical maps

(6.3)

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}},\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}

are strict quasi-isomorphisms. For the second map this follows from Corollary
4.32
.
For the first map, by Lemma
4.18
, it suffices to show that the canonical map

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{\breve{F}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.
But this holds because, by Corollary
4.32
, the canonical map
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(X)\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK},\breve{F}}(\widehat{X})
is a strict quasi-isomorphism.
∎

6.2.
From overconvergent syntomic cohomology to pro-étale cohomology

We will construct now a comparison morphism between geometric syntomic cohomology of a smooth dagger variety and its pro-étale cohomology. We will prove that it is a strict quasi-isomorphism in a stable range.

6.2.1.
Overconvergent geometric syntomic cohomology via presentations of dagger structures

We start with showing that the overconvergent geometric syntomic cohomology defined as in
[
20
, Sec. 6.3]
using presentations of dagger structures, a priori different from the overconvergent geometric syntomic cohomology defined as in
[
20
, Sec. 5.4]
, is strictly quasi-isomorphic to it. This was shown in
[
20
, Prop. 6.17]
in the arithmetic case, where the key ingredient of the proof is the comparison theorem between arithmetic overconvergent and rigid analytic syntomic cohomology of partially proper dagger spaces. We had to wait for the geometric version of the later comparison theorem (our Theorem
6.2
) to state the geometric analog of

[
20
, Prop. 6.17]
.

(i)
Local definition.
Let
X
be a dagger affinoid over
C
. Let
{\rm pres}(X)=\{X_{h}\}
. Recall that we have defined the syntomic cohomology

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad r\in{\bf N}.

We have a natural map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

(6.4)

\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}\colon{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

defined as the composition

(6.5)

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X^{\circ}_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X^{{\circ},\dagger}_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

The third quasi-isomorphism holds by Theorem
6.2
because
X^{\circ}_{h}
is partially proper.

(ii)
Globalization.
For a general smooth dagger variety
X
over
C
,
using the natural equivalence of analytic topoi

{\rm Sh}({\rm SmAff}^{\dagger}_{C,\operatorname{\acute{e}t}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm Sh}({\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C,\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}),

we define the sheaf
{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(r)
,
r\in{\bf N}
, on
X_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}
as the sheaf associated to the presheaf defined by:
U\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(U,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
,
U\in{\rm SmAff}^{\dagger}_{C}
,
U\to X
an étale map. We define
39
39

39

We will show below (see Proposition
6.6
) that this definition of
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
, for a smooth dagger affinoid
X
, gives an object naturally strictly quasi-isomorphic to the one defined above.
in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(r)),\quad r\in{\bf N}.

Globalizing the map
\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}
from (
6.4
)
we obtain a natural map

\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

(iii)
A comparison quasi-isomorphism.

Proposition 6.6
.

The above map
\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}
is a strict quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

By étale descent, we may assume that
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid.
Looking at the composition (
6.5
) defining the map
\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}
we see that it suffices to show that the natural map

\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X^{{\rm o},\dagger}_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. Or, from the definitions of both sides, that
we have strict quasi-isomorphisms in, resp.,
{\mathscr{D}}_{\varphi,N}(C_{\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}})
and
{\mathscr{D}}{\mathscr{F}}(C_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}})

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{{\rm o},\dagger}_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}),

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{{\rm o},\dagger}_{h}/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

We may assume that
X
is defined over a finite field extension
L
of
K
, i.e., there exists
X_{L}
such that
X\simeq X_{L,C}

Then the above maps factor as

\displaystyle\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X^{{\rm o},\dagger}_{h})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X_{h}))\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X^{{\rm o},\dagger}_{L,h})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X_{L,h}))\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{L}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}).

In the Hyodo-Kato case, the first map is a strict quasi-isomorphism by definition of the dagger tensor product. In the de Rham case, the first map is a strict filtered quasi-isomorphism by the computation (
3.35
).

∎

6.2.2.
The geometric overconvergent period map and a comparison result

We are now ready to define and study the overconvergent period map.
Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
,
r\geq 0
. Define the period
map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

(6.7)

\alpha_{r}:{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

as the composition

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\alpha_{r}^{\dagger}}}\,{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

where the first map is the map
\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}
from Proposition
6.6
and the second map is defined by globalizing the following map defined for a dagger affinoid
X
with the presentation
\{X_{h}\}
:

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\alpha_{r}}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

Here
\alpha_{r}
is the rigid analytic period map (see Proposition
3.1
).

We have the following compatibility with the rigid analytic period map:

Proposition 6.8
.

(Dagger-rigid analytic compatibility)
Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
and
r\geq 0
.

(1)

The following diagram

\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\alpha}_{r}}
\textstyle{{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))}

commutes.

(2)

If
X
is partially proper then the maps
\iota
and
\iota_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}
in the above diagram are strict quasi-isomorphisms.

Here, the period map
{\alpha}_{r}
is the one defined above.
We put hat above its rigid analytic analog to distinguish it from the dagger period map.

Proof.

For the first claim, it suffices to show that this diagram naturally commutes étale locally. So we may assume that
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid. Then checking commutativity is straightforward from the definitions.

For the second claim, note that the map
\iota
is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Theorem
6.2
and the map
\iota_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}
is a strict quasi-isomorphism by Proposition
3.1
, point 3a.
∎

The following comparison result follows almost immediately from its rigid analytic analog (see Proposition
3.1
, point 2c):

Theorem 6.9
.

For
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
and
r\geq 0
,
the period map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

(6.10)

\alpha_{r}:{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

is a strict quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.

Proof.

We may localize and assume that
X
is a dagger affinoid.
Proposition
3.1
yields immediately the strict quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r-1}
(since
L^{i}\operatorname{colim}
vanishes for
i>1
). It remains to show that the map
\alpha_{r}
induces an isomorphism on cohomology in degree
r
. For that, consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{r}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\zeta}
\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{r}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r+1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{r+1}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{r}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r+1)).}

The right vertical arrow is a multiplication by
p
-adic root of unity. The bottom arrow is an isomorphism by the above argument. The left vertical arrow is an isomorphism by the diagram in Proposition
5.20
and a chase of the diagram in
[
17
, Rem. 4.5]
(we note here that we do not need comparison with pro-étale cohomology for this chase).
It follows that the top horizontal map is an isomorphism, as wanted.
∎

The above theorem it implies the following result (which will be the starting point of our study of
C_{\operatorname{st}}
-conjecture for smooth analytic varieties in
[
21
]
):

Corollary 6.11
.

For
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
,
r\geq 0
, and
i\leq r
, we have the long exact sequence

\cdots\to\widetilde{H}^{i-1}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r})\to\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}})^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,\widetilde{H}^{i}({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r})

Here we set

H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:=\lim_{n}(H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}),

for an increasing covering
\{U_{n}\}_{n}
of
X
by quasi-compact open (note that the groups
H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n})
are of finite rank).

Proof.

Use Theorem
6.9
and the obvious fact that the canonical map
[H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\to[H^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(U_{n})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{{\bf B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}
is an isomorphism.
∎

7.
Geometrization of period morphisms

The purpose of this section is to geometrize syntomic cohomology (and the related Hyodo-Kato and de Rham cohomologies), pro-étale cohomology, and the associated period morphisms both in the rigid analytic and the overconvergent set-ups. By "geometrization" we mean putting a topological VS-structure. The key computation is the one showing that the rigid analytic version of Fontaine-Messing period morphism is a shadow of a VS-morphism. Both sides of the period morphism, crystalline syntomic cohomology and pro-étale cohomology, have natural VS structures. However it it not immediately clear that the period map navigates well between these two VS-structures. To show that, in fact, it does so we use the presentation of the period map via
(\varphi,\Gamma)
-modules introduced in
[
19
]
,
[
26
]
.

7.1.
Geometrization

In this section we explain how to geometrize the cohomologies and the period morphism (th.
7.3
).
In the next sections we prove Theorem
7.3
, first in the lifted case, then in the general case.

7.1.1.
Vector Spaces

A VS, resp. a VS
+
, is a functor from perfectoid affinoids
(\Lambda,\Lambda^{+})
over
(C,{\mathscr{O}}_{C})

(denoted by
\Lambda=(\Lambda,\Lambda^{+})
in what follows) to

{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules, resp.
{\bf Z}_{p}
-modules. If
{\mathbb{W}}
is a VS
+
, then
{\bf Q}_{p}\otimes_{{\bf Z}_{p}}{\mathbb{W}}

is a VS. VS’s form an abelian category.
Trivial examples of VS’s are:

\bullet

finite dimensional
{\bf Q}_{p}
-vector spaces
V
,
with associated functor
\Lambda\mapsto V
for all
\Lambda
,

\bullet

{\mathbb{V}}^{d}
, for
d\in{\bf N}
, with
{\mathbb{V}}^{d}(\Lambda)=\Lambda^{d}
, for
all
\Lambda
.

More interesting examples are provided by Fontaine’s rings
[
24
,
14
]
:

\bullet

{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cr}}^{+}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm st}}^{+}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cr}}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm st}}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}
are naturally VS’s (and even Rings).

\bullet
If
m\geq 1
, then
{\mathbb{B}}_{m}:={\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}/t^{m}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
is a VS (and also a Ring).

\bullet
Let
h\geq 1
and
d\in{\bf Z}
. Then
{\mathbb{U}}_{h,d}=({\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cr}}^{+})^{\varphi^{h}=p^{d}}
if
d\geq 0
,
and
{\mathbb{U}}_{h,d}={\mathbb{B}}_{d}/{\bf Q}_{p^{h}}
if
d<0
, are VS’s.

Exemples of VS
+
’s include
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm inf}
,
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}
, or
{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}
if
0<u\leq v
;
the last example being the functor sending
\Lambda
to the
p
-adic completion of
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm inf}(\Lambda)[\frac{p}{[\alpha]},\frac{[\beta]}{p}]
, where
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathscr{O}}_{C}^{\flat}
with
v(\alpha)=\frac{1}{v}

and
v(\beta)=\frac{1}{u}
.
By
[
26
, Prop. 3.2]
,
we have
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda)\subset{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({\Lambda})
, for
u\geq\frac{1}{p-1}
.
If
v\geq 1
, we have
{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)\subset{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(\Lambda)
and this inclusion
induces a filtration on
{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)
.

\bullet
The semistable period rings can be also lifted to VS’s. We set

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathbb{B}}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:=\widehat{{\mathbb{B}}}^{+}_{p,\operatorname{st}}:=({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}<t_{p}[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}-1>)^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}[\tfrac{1}{p}],\quad{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}:={\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{p,\operatorname{st}}:={\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}[\log([\widetilde{p}])],

\displaystyle\kappa:{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to\widehat{{\mathbb{B}}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}},\,\log([\widetilde{p}])\mapsto-\log(t_{p}[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}),\quad\iota:{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},\,\log([\widetilde{p}])\mapsto-\log(p[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}),

\displaystyle\iota:\widehat{{\mathbb{B}}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}\to{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}},\,t_{p}[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}\mapsto p[\widetilde{p}]^{-1}.

If
{\mathbb{W}}
is one of the above Rings, we denote by
{\bf W}
the ring
{\mathbb{W}}(C)
: for
example
{\bf A}^{[u,v]}={\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(C)
(for the other Rings
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm inf}
,
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cr}}^{+}
,
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
, etc.,
one gets back the rings already defined).

Remark 7.1
.

The above definition gives presheaves on
{\rm Perf}_{C}
. Passing to the associated sheaves
gives a natural viewpoint on VS’s and VS
+
’s; this was put to use by Le Bras in his thesis
[
35
]
.

7.1.2.
Pro-étale cohomology

Let
X
be a smooth rigid analytic variety over
C
.
If
\Lambda
is a perfectoid
C
-Banach algebra, let
X_{\Lambda}
be the scalar extension
X\times_{C}\Lambda
.
The functor

\Lambda\mapsto H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p})
defines a VS.
That is, there exists a VS

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
such that

{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})(\Lambda)=H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p})
,
for all perfectoid
C
-Banach algebras.
In particular,
H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
is the space of
C
-points
of
{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
; we have put in this way a geometric structure on

H^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})
.

We will use a bit more general
40
40

40

That is, presheaves on
{\rm Perf}_{C}
with values in different categories than that of
{\bf Q}_{p}
-modules.
VS’s:

(1)

the cohomology complex: the functor

{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p}):\quad\Lambda\mapsto{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p})

defines a VS with values in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
;

(2)

its cohomology groups
\widetilde{{\mathbb{H}}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p})
form a VS with values in
LH(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
;

(3)

its algebraic cohomology groups
{{\mathbb{H}}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p})
form the VS described above. We have a natural map
\widetilde{{\mathbb{H}}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p})\to{{\mathbb{H}}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{\Lambda},{\bf Q}_{p})
.

7.1.3.
Crystalline syntomic cohomology

To geometrize (filtered) absolute crystalline cohomology, we define the functor

(7.2)

\mathbb{F}^{r}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{cr}}(X):\quad\Lambda\mapsto F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{R}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cr}}^{+}(\Lambda),\quad r\geq 0,

that lifts the absolute crystalline cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)
from Section
3.2.2
. The tensor product used in (
7.2
) needs to be defined. We do it in the following way. We set

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{R}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm cr}}^{+}(\Lambda):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr},\Lambda}),

where
F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}_{\operatorname{cr},\Lambda}
is the
\eta
-étale sheafification
41
41

41

We do not discuss local-global compatibilities. As far as we can tell this does not cause problems.

on
{\mathscr{M}}^{\rm ss}_{C}
of the presheaf

{\mathscr{X}}\mapsto(F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda))_{{\bf Q}_{p}}
.

We proceed similarly for rigid
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology
(from Section
3.3.1
): we define the functor

{\mathbb{F}}^{r}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}):\quad\Lambda\mapsto F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(\Lambda),\quad r\geq 0,

that lifts the filtered
{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}
-cohomology
F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})
. Here

F^{r}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}(\Lambda):={\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{\acute{e}t}}(X,F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\operatorname{cr}},\Lambda}),

where
F^{r}{\mathscr{A}}^{\underset{\widehat{\phantom{\ \ }}}{\phantom{\_}}}_{{\operatorname{cr}},\Lambda}
is the
\eta
-étale sheafification on
{\mathscr{M}}^{\rm ss}_{C}
of the presheaf

{\mathscr{X}}\mapsto{\mathrm{R}}\operatorname{lim}_{i\geq r}(({\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{J}}^{[r]})/F^{i})\widehat{\otimes}^{\mathrm{L}}_{({\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}/F^{i})}({\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda)/F^{i}))_{{\bf Q}_{p}}.

Finally, we lift crystalline syntomic cohomology by setting

\mathbb{R}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)):\quad\Lambda\mapsto[{\mathbb{F}}^{r}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{cr}}(X)(\Lambda)_{{\bf Q}_{p}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\varphi-p^{r}}}\,{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})(\Lambda)/F^{r}].

7.1.4.
Rigid analytic varieties, period morphism

We move now to the geometrization of rigid analytic period morphisms.
We will prove the following theorem.

Theorem 7.3
.

For
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
and
r\geq 0
, the functorial period map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\alpha_{r}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

lifts to a functorial map of VS’s (with values in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
):

{\bbalpha}_{r}:\quad{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

which is a strict quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.

The next two sections are devoted to the proof of this theorem.

7.2.
Local period morphism, lifted case

We start by defining
{\bbalpha}_{r}(\Lambda)
locally, in the simplest of cases.

7.2.1.
Coordinates

Consider a frame

(7.4)

R^{+}_{\Box}:={\mathscr{O}}_{C}\{X,\tfrac{1}{X_{0}\ldots X_{a}},\tfrac{\varpi}{X_{a+1}\ldots X_{d}}\},\quad R_{\Box}=R^{+}_{\Box}[\tfrac{1}{p}],

where
X=(X_{0},\ldots,X_{d})
and
\varpi\in{\mathscr{O}}_{C}\setminus{\mathscr{O}}_{C}^{*}
, and a formal scheme
{\mathscr{X}}=\operatorname{Spf}R^{+}
, for an algebra
R^{+}
, which is the
p
-adic completion of an étale algebra over
R^{+}_{\Box}
.
We equip
\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+}_{\Box})
and
\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+})
with the logarithmic structure
42
42

42

Note that we do not allow horizontal divisors at
\infty
.
induced by the special fiber.

For
m\geq 0
, define

R^{m,+}_{\Box}:={\mathscr{O}}_{C}\{X^{{1}/{p^{m}}},\tfrac{1}{(X_{0}\ldots X_{a})^{{1}/{p^{m}}}},\tfrac{\varpi^{{1}/{p^{m}}}}{(X_{a+1}\ldots X_{d})^{{1}/{p^{m}}}}\},\quad R^{m}_{\Box}=R^{m,+}_{\Box}[\tfrac{1}{p}],

and set
R^{\infty,+}_{\Box}
equal to the
p
-adic completion of
\operatorname{colim}_{m}R^{m,+}_{\Box}
.
Let

R^{m,+}:=R^{m,+}_{\Box}\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{+}_{\Box}}R^{+},\quad R^{\infty,+}:=R^{\infty,+}_{\Box}\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{+}_{\Box}}R^{+},\quad R^{m}=R^{m,+}[\tfrac{1}{p}],\quad R:=R^{0},\quad R^{\infty}=R^{\infty,+}[\tfrac{1}{p}],

so that
R^{\infty}
is a perfectoid Banach algebra.
Define
\Gamma_{R}:=\operatorname{Gal}(R^{\infty}/R)
. We have
\Gamma_{R}\simeq{\bf Z}_{p}^{d}
.

Choose
\varpi^{\flat}\in{\mathscr{O}}_{C}^{\flat}
with
\theta([\varpi^{\flat}])=\varpi
. We define

R^{+}_{\inf,\Box}:={\bf A}_{\inf}\{X,\tfrac{1}{X_{0}\ldots X_{a}},\tfrac{[{\varpi}^{\flat}]}{X_{a+1}\ldots X_{d}}\}

and lift the map
R^{+}_{\Box}\to R^{+}
to an étale map
R^{+}_{\inf,\Box}\to R^{+}_{\inf}
.
Set

R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}:=R^{+}_{\inf}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}_{\inf}}{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}},\quad R^{[u,v]}:=R^{+}_{\inf}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}_{\inf}}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}.

Endow everything with the log-structure coming from
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}
and
\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+})
.
This gives us the commutative diagram (with cartesian squares)

\textstyle{\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+}_{\Box})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr},\Box})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Spf}({\mathscr{O}}_{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Spf}({\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}})}

We equip
\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}})
with the (unique) lift
\varphi
of the canonical Frobenius on
\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr},\Box})

(induced by
\varphi
on
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}
and by
X_{i}\mapsto X_{i}^{p}
,
0\leq i\leq d
).

We define the filtrations
F^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
on
R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}

and
F^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}R^{[u,v]}
on
R^{[u,v]}
by inducing them from
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}

and
{\bf A}^{[u,v]}
. We have the corresponding filtered de Rham complex

F^{r}\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}:=F^{r}R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\to F^{r-1}R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}\Omega^{1}_{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}\to F^{r-2}R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}\Omega^{2}_{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}\cdots

The crystalline syntomic cohomology
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}({\mathscr{X}},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))
is computed by the complex

{\rm Syn}(R^{+},r)_{{\bf Q}_{p}},

where

{\rm Syn}(R^{+},r):=[F^{r}\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{p^{r}-\varphi}}\,\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}].

This follows from the fact that the (filtered) absolute crystalline cohomology
F^{i}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}})\simeq F^{i}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{cr}}({\mathscr{X}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}})
.

The key ingredient to turn the complex coming from the fundamental exact sequence
(i.e.,

is not functorial enough),

).

We set

Here, all the quasi-morphisms are

-quasi-isomorphisms (after truncation

Moreover:

•

G
and
\Gamma
are
G_{R}
and
\Gamma_{R}
;

•

C_{G},C_{\Gamma}
denote the complexes of continuous cochains on the groups
G,\Gamma
, respectively;

\bullet

K
denotes a complex of Koszul type:

—

the indices indicate the operators involved in the complex:

\diamond

\partial

is a shorthand for
\big(X_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial X_{1}},\dots,X_{d}\frac{\partial}{\partial X_{d}}\big)
,

\diamond

\Gamma
is a shorthand for
(\gamma_{1}-1,\dots,\gamma_{d}-1)
, where the
\gamma_{i}
’s are our
chosen topological generators of
\Gamma
,

\diamond

\operatorname{Lie}\Gamma
is a shorthand for
\big(\nabla_{1},\dots,\nabla_{d})
, where
\nabla_{i}=\log\gamma_{i}
,
so that the
\nabla_{i}
’s are a basis of
\operatorname{Lie}\Gamma
over
{\bf Z}_{p}
,

\diamond

\varphi
is a shorthand for
\varphi-p^{r}
.

—

only the first term of the complex is indicated: the rest is implicit and obtained from the first term
so that the maps involved make sense:
\varphi
does not respect filtration or annulus of convergence,
and
\partial
decrease the degrees of filtration by
1
.

For example, choosing a basis of
\Omega_{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}
transforms complexes involving differentials
into complexes of Koszul type:
K_{\partial,\varphi}(F^{r}S)
if
S=R^{+}_{{\operatorname{cr}}}

or
S=R^{[u,v]}
.

Let us now turn our attention to the maps between rows:

\bullet

Going from the first row to the second row just uses the injections
R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\subset R^{[u,v]}
, etc.

\bullet

Going from the third row to the second row: the map
\mu_{H}
is the inflation map from
\Gamma_{R}
to
G_{R_{\Lambda}}
, using the injection

R^{\infty}\subset\overline{R}
(we use almost étale descent – i.e., Faltings’ almost purity
theorem or its extension by Scholze or Kedlaya-Liu – to prove that it is a quasi-isomorphism); the other map is a "change of Lie algebra map"
t^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
appearing
in the proof of
[
26
, Lemma 5.7]
(multiplication by suitable powers of
t
).

\bullet

Going from the fourth row to the third row:
uses the injection of
R^{[u,v]}\hookrightarrow{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{R^{\infty}}
from (
7.5
);
the map
{\mathscr{L}}\hskip-1.42271pt{az}
is defined as in
[
26
, Lemma 5.8]
.

Let us now describe the maps between columns:

\bullet

The bottom map from the first column to the second one is the map connecting
continuous cohomology of
\Gamma_{R}
to Koszul complex.

\bullet

by the canonical injection of rings; it is a

-quasi-isomorphisms by

.

Here, all the quasi-morphisms are
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphisms (after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
).

The arrow is plain if it is very similar to the one appearing in diagram (
7.15
) and dotted if it requires
additional arguments.

Moreover (we indicate only differences with diagram (
7.15
)):

•

tensor products with
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda)
(resp
{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)
) are over

{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}
(resp.
{\bf A}^{[u,v]}
);

•

(R^{\infty}_{\Lambda},R^{\infty,+}_{\Lambda})=(R^{\infty},R^{\infty,+})\widehat{\otimes}_{(C,{\mathscr{O}}_{C})}(\Lambda,\Lambda^{+})
; it is a perfectoid affinoid by
[
43
, Prop. 6.18]
;

•

G
and
\Gamma
are
G_{R_{\Lambda}}
and
\Gamma_{R}
;

Let us now turn our attention to the maps between rows:

\bullet

The plain arrows are induced by the analogous maps in diagram (
7.15
) They are
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphisms by the same argument as in loc. cit. since tensoring with
{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({\Lambda})

can be done outside the quasi-isomorphic complexes. We note that the tensor products

\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}^{[u,v]}}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({\Lambda})
are completed but not, a priori, derived. This does not cause problems because
the
{\bf A}_{\inf}
-module

{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm inf}(\Lambda)
is flat:
{\mathscr{O}}_{C}^{\flat}
is a valuation ring hence the
{\mathscr{O}}_{C}^{\flat}
-module
\Lambda^{+,\flat}
, being torsion free, is flat.

\bullet

Going from the third row to the second row: the map
\mu_{H}
is the inflation map from
\Gamma_{R}
to
G_{R_{\Lambda}}
, using the injection

R^{\infty}_{\Lambda}\subset\overline{R}_{\Lambda}
.
We use almost étale descent (i.e., Faltings’ almost purity
theorem or its extension by Scholze or Kedlaya-Liu) to prove that it is a quasi-isomorphism. The map
t^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
is the multiplication by suitable powers of
t
(we use here Lemma
7.11
).

Finally, the maps
\beta,\delta
in the diagram are treated by Lemma
7.17
below.
∎

Lemma 7.17
.

(1)

The canonical morphism

\tau_{\leq r}\beta:\tau_{\leq r}K_{\partial,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda))_{n}{\to}\tau_{\leq r}K_{\partial,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{[u,v]}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}^{[u,v]}}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({\Lambda}))_{n}

is a
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphism.

(2)

The canonical morphisms

\delta:F^{r}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{R^{\infty}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}^{[u,v]}}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({\Lambda})\to F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({R^{\infty}_{\Lambda}})

are isomorphisms.

Proof.

For the first claim,
set
R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr},\Lambda}:=R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda)
.
This ring has the same form as
R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
(see Section
7.2
) but with
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}

replaced by
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda)
. The above morphism can be written as

\tau_{\leq r}K_{\partial,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr},\Lambda})_{n}{\to}\tau_{\leq r}K_{\partial,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr},\Lambda}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\bf A}^{[u,v]})_{n}.

Now, the proof in
[
26
, Sec. 4.1]
goes through verbatim by changing
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}
to
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda)
.

For the second claim of the lemma, by Lemma
7.11
, we can replace the filtration by the one given by powers of
t
. Hence, it is enough to show that the canonical map

(7.18)

{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm inf}(R^{\infty})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}_{\inf}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm inf}(\Lambda)\to{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm inf}(R^{\infty}_{\Lambda})

is an isomorphism (the passage to
[u,v]
-version is obtained by taking the completed tensor product of (
7.18
) with
{\bf A}^{[u,v]}
) . Or, since both sides are
p
-adically derived complete,
that so is its reduction modulo
p
:

R^{\infty,+,\flat}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{C}^{\flat}}\Lambda^{+,\flat}\to R^{\infty,+,\flat}_{\Lambda}.

But this can be checked modulo
p^{\flat}
. That is, we want the canonical map

(R^{\infty,+,\flat}/p^{\flat})\otimes_{{\mathscr{O}}_{C}^{\flat}/p^{\flat}}(\Lambda^{+,\flat}/p^{\flat})\to R^{\infty,+,\flat}_{\Lambda}/p^{\flat}

to be an isomorphism.

Now, this map identifies with the canonical map

(R^{\infty,+}/p)\otimes_{{\mathscr{O}}_{C}/p}(\Lambda^{+}/p)\to R^{\infty,+}_{\Lambda}/p.

It suffices thus to show that the canonical map

R^{\infty,+}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathscr{O}}_{C}}\Lambda^{+}\to R^{\infty,+}_{\Lambda}

is an isomorphism. But this is clear since both sides are isomorphic to the completion of the same étale extension of the tower

\Lambda^{+}\{X^{{1}/{p^{\infty}}},\tfrac{1}{(X_{1}\ldots X_{a})^{{1}/{p^{\infty}}}},\tfrac{\varpi^{{1}/{p^{\infty}}}}{(X_{a+1}\ldots X_{d})^{{1}/{p^{\infty}}}}\}.

∎

7.2.5.
Modification of the period morphism
{\alpha}^{R^{+}}_{r,n}(\Lambda)

The Fontaine-Messing period morphism

{\alpha}^{R^{+}}_{r,n}(\Lambda):\quad{\rm Syn}(R^{+},r)_{n}(\Lambda)\to C(G_{R_{\Lambda}},{\bf Z}/p^{n}(r)^{\prime})

constructed above is neither functorial in
R^{+}
nor in
\Lambda^{+}
. By diagram (
7.16
), we can replace it by the map that traces that diagram down-bottom right-up and replaces
C_{G}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}(\overline{R}_{\Lambda}))
with

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\rm Spa}(R_{\Lambda}),K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}))
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{A}}^{R^{+}}_{r,n}(\Lambda):{\rm Syn}(R^{+},r)_{n}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\simeq K_{\partial,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda))_{n}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\tau_{\leq r}\beta}K_{\partial,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{[u,v]}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{t^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},\tau_{\leq r}}K_{\operatorname{Lie}\Gamma,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{[u,v]}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n}\xleftarrow[\sim]{\mathscr{L}az}K_{\Gamma,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{[u,v]}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{}C_{\Gamma}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}R^{[u,v]}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n})\xrightarrow[\sim]{\varepsilon}C_{\Gamma}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{{R}^{\infty}}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\delta}C_{\Gamma}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({{R}^{\infty}_{\Lambda}}))_{n})\xrightarrow[\sim]{\nu}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\rm Spa}(R_{\Lambda}),K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}})_{n})

\displaystyle\xleftarrow[\sim]{\rm FES}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\rm Spa}(R_{\Lambda}),{\bf Z}/p^{n}(r)^{\prime}).

Here
{\rm FES}
stands for "fundamental exact sequence".
This map is functorial in
\Lambda
and is a
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphism, for a universal constant
c
, after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
(by Proposition
7.14
). In the next section we will modify it to make it functorial in
R^{+}
as well.

.

) is a

-quasi-isomorphism, for a universal constant

, after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the first and the second map in the composition (

) are
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphisms, for universal constants
c
, after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
. Consider the product

\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}}\widetilde{R}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}})
(we put
\widetilde{\phantom{X}}
to distinguish diagram (

) from diagram (

)) and the canonical closed immersion

. Let

) for

. Consider the compatible maps

\displaystyle p_{1}:\operatorname{Spf}({\mathscr{D}}^{+})\to\operatorname{Spf}({\mathscr{D}}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}),\quad p_{2}:\operatorname{Spf}({\mathscr{D}}^{+})\to\operatorname{Spf}(\widetilde{R}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}),

\displaystyle p_{1}:\operatorname{Spf}({{\bf E}}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R}})\to\operatorname{Spf}({{\bf E}}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R},\kappa}),\quad p_{2}:\operatorname{Spf}({{\bf E}}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R}})\to\operatorname{Spf}(\widetilde{{\bf E}}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R}})

and

, respectively.
These maps are also compatible with the other maps in diagrams (

).
They induce compatible maps

(7.24)

\displaystyle p_{2}^{*}:F^{r}\Omega_{\widetilde{R}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr},n}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}F^{r}\Omega_{{\mathscr{D}}^{+}_{n}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},\quad p^{*}_{2}:F^{r}\Omega_{\widetilde{{\bf E}}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R},n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}F^{r}\Omega_{{\bf E}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R},n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},

–maps are also compatible with the canonical maps from

Now, since the maps in (

) for

and, then, with the ones for

-quasi-isomorphisms, for a universal constant
c
, after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}

∎
