Lemma 7.28
.

The period map (
7.26
) is a
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphism, for a universal constant
c
, after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the first and second maps in the composition (
7.27
) are
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphisms, for a universal constant
c
, after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.
But since the map
\widetilde{\pi}
in diagram (
7.12
) is log-smooth
(we put
\widetilde{\phantom{X}}
to distinguish diagram (
7.12
) from diagram (
7.25
)) and
{\mathscr{D}}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
in diagram (
7.25
) is
I
-adically complete, for the defining PD-ideal
I
,
we have maps from
f:\operatorname{Spf}({\mathscr{D}}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}})
to
\operatorname{Spf}(\widetilde{R}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}})
and from
\operatorname{Spf}({\bf E}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R}_{\Lambda},\kappa})
to
\operatorname{Spf}(\widetilde{{\bf E}}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R}_{\Lambda}})
that are also compatible with with other maps in diagrams (
7.19
) and (
7.12
). These maps induces two compatible maps

\displaystyle F^{r}\Omega_{\widetilde{R}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr},n}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda)_{n}

\displaystyle\to F^{r}\Omega_{{\mathscr{D}}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr},n}/{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr},n}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda)_{n},

\displaystyle F^{r}\Omega_{\widetilde{{\bf E}}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R}_{\Lambda},n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}

\displaystyle\to F^{r}\Omega_{{\bf E}^{\rm PD}_{\overline{R}_{\Lambda},\kappa,n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}.

These maps are quasi-isomorphisms: the first one by the first quasi-isomorphism from (
7.24
) and flatness of
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda)
over
{\bf A}_{\operatorname{cr}}
; the second one, via the filtered Poincaré Lemma (note that both the domain and the target compute crystalline cohomology of
\overline{R}^{+}_{\Lambda,n}
over
{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\overline{R}_{\Lambda})_{n}
), can be identified with the identity map

\operatorname{Id}:F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\overline{R}_{\Lambda})_{n}\to F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\overline{R}_{\Lambda})_{n}.

Assume now that the map
f
is compatible with Frobenius. Then in our lemma we may take

R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}=\widetilde{R}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
in which case we can use Proposition
7.14
. In general, map
f
will not be compatible with Frobenius and then we have to argue via a zig-zag of such maps as in the proof of Lemma
7.22
.
∎

7.3.3.
Modification of the period morphism
{\alpha}^{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}_{r,n}(\Lambda)

As in Section
7.2
, we can replace the Fontaine-Messing period morphism

{\alpha}^{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}_{r,n}(\Lambda):\quad{\rm Syn}(R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}},r)_{n}(\Lambda)\to C(G_{R_{\Lambda}},{\bf Z}/p^{n}(r)^{\prime})

constructed above (which is neither functorial in
R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}
nor in
\Lambda^{+}
) by a better behaved morphism. But before doing this we need to make a special choice for our coordinate system (a simpler variant of the one used
50
50

50

There are notable differences: we did not separate the torus data and we allowed coordinates
R^{\Box}_{\delta}
which do not satisfy point (2) below.
in
[
13
, Sec. 5.17]
).

Assume that each pair of irreducible components of the special fiber of
{\mathscr{X}}
has nontrivial intersection (in particular,
{\mathscr{X}}
is connected) and that we have a closed immersion

(7.29)

\iota_{\Delta}:{\mathscr{X}}=\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+})\hookrightarrow\prod_{\delta\in\Delta}\operatorname{Spf}R^{\Box}_{\delta},

such that

(1)

R^{\Box}_{\delta}:={\mathscr{O}}_{C}\{X^{\pm 1}_{\delta,0},\ldots,X^{\pm 1}_{\delta,a_{\delta}},X_{\delta,{a_{\delta}+1}},\ldots,X_{d_{\delta}}\}/(X_{\delta,{a_{\delta}+1}}\cdots X_{d_{\delta}}-p^{b_{\delta}})
, where
\delta\in\Delta
, for a finite set
\Delta
,
and
b_{\delta}\in{\bf Q}_{\geq 0}
;

(2)

There exists a
\delta_{0}\in\Delta
such that the morphism
\operatorname{Spf}(R^{+})\to\operatorname{Spf}(R^{\Box}_{\delta_{0}})
is étale.

We set

\operatorname{Spf}(R^{\Box}_{\Delta}):=\prod_{\delta\in\Delta}\operatorname{Spf}R^{\Box}_{\delta}.

The formal schemes
\operatorname{Spf}R^{\Box}_{\delta}
s and
\operatorname{Spf}(R^{\Box}_{\Delta})
are endowed with the log-structures coming from the special fiber.

Let
R^{\Box,\infty}_{\delta}
be the
p
-adic completion of the ring

\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\mathscr{O}}_{C}\{X^{\pm\frac{1}{p^{n}}}_{\delta,0}\cdots X^{\pm\frac{1}{p^{n}}}_{\delta,a_{\delta}},X^{\frac{1}{p^{n}}}_{\delta,{a_{\delta}+1}},\ldots,X^{\frac{1}{p^{n}}}_{d_{\delta}}\}/(X^{\frac{1}{p^{n}}}_{\delta,{a_{\delta}+1}}\cdots X^{\frac{1}{p^{n}}}_{d_{\delta}}-p^{\frac{b_{\delta}}{p^{n}}}).

We denote by
R^{\Box,\infty}_{\Delta}
the completed tensor product of the above rings and set
R^{+,\infty}_{\Delta}:=R^{\Box,\infty}_{\Delta}\widehat{\otimes}_{R^{\Box}_{\Delta}}R^{+}
,
R^{\infty}_{\Delta}:=R^{+,\infty}_{\Delta}[1/p]
.
We consider the groups

Lemma 7.32
.

The maps

\displaystyle\varepsilon:C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}R_{\Delta}^{[u,v]}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)))\to C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{R_{\Delta}^{\infty}}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))),

\displaystyle\delta:C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{R_{\Delta}^{\infty}}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)))\to C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({{R}^{\infty}_{\Delta,\Lambda}})))

are
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphisms after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.

Proof.

We will pass to the frame
R^{\Box}_{\delta_{0}}
, where the statement of the lemma is known. For the first map, arguing as in the proof of Lemma
7.28
, we find a map
f:\operatorname{Spf}(R^{\rm PD}_{\Delta})\to\operatorname{Spf}(\widetilde{R}_{\operatorname{cr}})
compatible with the diagram (
7.30
) and the analog of the diagram (
7.12
) for the frame
R^{\Box}_{\delta_{0}}
. If this map is compatible with Frobenius then it induces the vertical arrows in the commutative diagram:

\textstyle{C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}R_{\Delta}^{[u,v]}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon}
\textstyle{C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{R_{\Delta}^{\infty}}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)))}
\textstyle{C_{\Gamma_{\delta_{0}}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}\widetilde{R}_{\delta_{0}}^{[u,v]}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{\delta_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\leq r}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{C_{\Gamma_{\delta_{0}}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{R_{\delta_{0}}^{\infty}}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The map
\varepsilon_{\delta_{0}}
is a
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphism by diagram (
7.16
), the left vertical map is a
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphism (after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
)
because both the domain and the target compute
{\rm Syn}(R^{+},r)_{n}(\Lambda)
, and the right vertical map is an almost quasi-isomorphism by almost étale descent.
It follows that the map
\varepsilon
from our lemma is a
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\geq r}
, as wanted. In general, the map
f
is not compatible with Frobenius and we have to proceed by a zig-zag as in the proof of Lemma
7.22
.

For the map
\delta
, consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{R_{\Delta}^{\infty}}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{C_{G}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({\overline{R}_{\Lambda}})))}
\textstyle{C_{\Gamma_{\delta_{0}}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{R_{\delta_{0}}^{\infty}}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{H}\delta}
\scriptstyle{\sim}

The diagonal map is a
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphism by the diagram (
7.16
). It follows that so is the horizontal map and then the map
\delta
, as wanted.
∎

Remark 7.33
.

The reader will probably notice that for what follows we did not need to prove Lemma
7.32
: it will suffice to know that the composition
\mu_{H}\delta\varepsilon
is a, truncated at
r
,

p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphism and this we know since the diagram (
7.31
) commutes and the top horizontal maps are truncated at
r

p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphisms.

Diagram (
7.31
) allows us to replace
{\alpha}^{R^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}_{r,n}(\Lambda)
with the map
{\bbalpha}^{R^{\Box}_{\Delta}}_{r,n}(\Lambda)
that traces that diagram down-bottom right-up and replaces
C_{G}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}(\overline{R}_{\Lambda}))
with

{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\rm Spa}(R_{\Lambda}),K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}}))
:

\displaystyle{\bbalpha}^{R^{\Box}_{\Delta}}_{r,n}(\Lambda):{\rm Syn}({R^{\Box}_{\Delta}},r)_{n}(\Lambda)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\rm Spa}(R_{\Lambda}),{\bf Z}/p^{n}(r)^{\prime}).

\displaystyle{\bbalpha}^{R^{\Box}_{\Delta}}_{r,n}(\Lambda):{\rm Syn}({R^{\Box}_{\Delta}},r)_{n}(\Lambda)

\displaystyle\simeq K_{\partial,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{\rm PD}_{\Delta}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}_{\rm cr}(\Lambda))_{n}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\tau_{\leq r}\beta}K_{\partial,\varphi}(F^{r}R^{[u,v]}_{\Delta}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{t^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},\tau_{\leq r}}K_{\operatorname{Lie}\Gamma_{\Delta},\varphi}(F^{r}R^{[u,v]}_{\Delta}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n}\xleftarrow[\sim]{\mathscr{L}az}K_{\Gamma_{\Delta},\varphi}(F^{r}R^{[u,v]}_{\Delta}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{}C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}R^{[u,v]}_{\Delta}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n})\xrightarrow[\sim]{\varepsilon}C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\bf A}^{[u,v]}_{{R}^{\infty}_{\Delta}}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}(\Lambda))_{n})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\delta}C_{\Gamma_{\Delta}}(K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}^{[u,v]}({{R}^{\infty}_{\Delta,\Lambda}}))_{n})\xrightarrow[\sim]{\nu}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\rm Spa}(R_{\Lambda}),K_{\varphi}(F^{r}{\mathbb{A}}_{\operatorname{cr}})_{n})

\displaystyle\xleftarrow[\sim]{\rm FES}{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\rm Spa}(R_{\Lambda}),{\bf Z}/p^{n}(r)^{\prime}).

Here
{\rm FES}
stands for "fundamental exact sequence".
This map is functorial in
\Lambda
and is a
p^{cr}
-quasi-isomorphism, for a universal constant
c
, after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
(by the discussion below diagram
7.31
). Hence its rational version

{\bbalpha}^{R^{\Box}_{\Delta}}_{r}(\Lambda):{\rm Syn}({R^{\Box}_{\Delta}},r)_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\Lambda)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\rm Spa}(R_{\Lambda}),{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

is functorial in
\Lambda
and a strict quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
.

The map
{\bbalpha}^{R^{\Box}_{\Delta}}_{r}(\Lambda)
is functorial in the triples
(R^{+},{R^{\Box}_{\Delta}},\iota_{\Delta})
and taking the colimit over the filtered system of such embeddings with fixed
R^{+}
we get a map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

(7.34)

{\bbalpha}^{R^{+}}_{r}(\Lambda):{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}({\rm Spf}(R^{+}),r)_{{\bf Q}_{p}}(\Lambda)\to{\mathrm{R}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}({\rm Spa}(R_{\Lambda}),{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

which is functorial with respect to
R^{+}
and
\Lambda
and a strict quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq n}
.

7.3.4.
Proof of Theorem
7.3

Let now
X\in{\rm Sm}_{C}
. The definition (
7.34
) of the period map globalizes using
\eta
-étale sheafification to a period map in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

{\bbalpha}_{r}(\Lambda):\quad{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))(\Lambda)\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))(\Lambda),\quad r\geq 0,

which for
\Lambda=C
recovers the period map of Fontaine-Messing.
This is a strict quasi-isomorphism after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
. Now, varying
\Lambda
we get the map we wanted:

{\bbalpha}_{r}:\quad{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad r\geq 0.

7.4.
Dagger varieties

We will now geometrize cohomologies and period morphisms associated to dagger varieties.

7.4.1.
Cohomologies

Let
X
be a dagger affinoid over
C
, and
\{X_{h}\}
be a presentation. Define the VS:

{\mathbb{R}}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}):=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{n}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}).

For a smooth dagger variety
X
over
C
, this globalizes, via étale sheafification, to the VS

{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}).

We set

\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}),{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}):\quad\Lambda\mapsto\widetilde{H}^{i}({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})(\Lambda)),{H}^{i}({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p})(\Lambda)).

We define similarly the Hyodo-Kato cohomology, the
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}
-cohomology, and the syntomic cohomology:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X),\quad\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X);\quad{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+}),\quad\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+});\quad{\mathbb{R}}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{\dagger,i}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r));

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),\quad\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

We note that the Hyodo-Kato cohomology is the constant functor equal to
{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{HK}}(X),\widetilde{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)
.

7.4.2.
Period maps

Let
X
be a dagger affinoid over
C
, and
\{X_{h}\}
be a presentation.
Let
r\geq 0
.
The local period morphisms of VS’s (with values in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
)

\displaystyle{\bbalpha}^{\dagger}_{r}:\quad{\mathbb{R}}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

are defined as

{\mathbb{R}}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\bbalpha}_{r,h}}}\,\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))={\mathbb{R}}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

For a smooth dagger variety
X
, this globalizes to period morphisms

{\bbalpha}^{\dagger}_{r}:\quad{\mathbb{R}}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

These are strict quasi-isomorphisms after truncation
\tau_{\leq r}
because so are the rigid analytic period morphisms
{\bbalpha}_{r,h}
by Theorem
7.3
.

Recall now that, for
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
, the period morphisms in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})

\alpha_{r}:\quad{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

are defined as the compositions

{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\xleftarrow[\sim]{\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}}{\rm R}\Gamma^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha^{\dagger}_{r}}}{{\to}}{\rm R}\Gamma_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).

These morphisms lift to VS. Indeed, it remains to show that we can lift the map
\iota^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}
to a map

{\bbiota}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}:\quad{\mathbb{R}}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r)),

and that this map is a strict quasi-isomorphism. We define the map
{\bbiota}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}}
by étale sheafifying the following composition (
X
is a smooth dagger affinoid over
C
),

\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}^{\dagger}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))=\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X^{\rm o}_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))}
\textstyle{{\phantom{XXXXXXXX}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L}\operatorname{colim}_{h}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X^{{\rm o},\dagger}_{h},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\bbiota}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

Here, the morphism
{\bbiota}
needs to be defined and both it and
the bottom morphism need to be shown to be strict quasi-isomorphisms.

Proposition 7.35
.

(Definition of the map
{\bbiota}
)

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
. We have a natural map of VS’s (with values in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
)

{\bbiota}:\quad{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

It is a strict quasi-isomorphism for
X
partially proper.

Proof.

We will set
{\bbiota}:={\bbiota}_{2}{\bbiota}_{1}
, with the maps
{\bbiota}_{1},{\bbiota}_{2}
defined as follows.

(i)
The map
{\bbiota}_{1}
.
The map
{\bbiota}_{1}
is defined as the following composition:

\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\big[[{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(X)\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(X/{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})/F^{r}\big]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\big[[{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\widehat{\mathbb{B}}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})/F^{r})\big]}
\textstyle{\big[[{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})_{F}\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0,\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}}\,{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})/F^{r})\big]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

It is a strict quasi-isomorphism.
Indeed, for that it suffices to show that the canonical map

[{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\to[{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}\widehat{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}

is a strict quasi-isomorphism. But this can be shown exactly as in Section
6.1.1
.

(ii)
The map
{\bbiota}_{2}
.

Now, we define a natural strict quasi-isomorphism

\textstyle{{\phantom{XXXX}[[{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{cr}}(\widehat{X})]^{\varphi=p^{r}}\smash{\mathop{\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\longrightarrow}\limits^{\operatorname{can}}}\,{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{cr}}(\widehat{X})/F^{r}]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(\widehat{X},{\bf Q}_{p}(r)).}

For that, it suffices to define the maps

and

(7.36)

\textstyle{[{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{HK}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}_{F^{\operatorname{nr}}}{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon^{\operatorname{HK}}_{\operatorname{st}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm BK}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\operatorname{HK}}\otimes\iota}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{dR}}(\widehat{X}/{\mathbb{B}}_{{\rm dR}}^{+})\widehat{\otimes}^{{\mathrm{R}}}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}}}{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{dR}})/F^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\rm BK}^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{[{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{cr}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{st}}]^{N=0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa\otimes\iota}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{cr}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{({\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{cr}}(\widehat{X})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\bf B}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}}}{\mathbb{B}}^{+}_{\operatorname{cr}})/F^{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\kappa\otimes\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}

We define the maps
\iota_{\rm BK}^{1}
and
\iota_{\rm BK}^{2}
to make the left and the right triangles in the diagram commute. They are strict quasi-isomorphisms. The remaining pieces of the diagram commute by definition.
∎

Let
X\in{\rm Sm}^{\dagger}_{C}
. We define the global period morphism of VS’s (with values in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
)

{\bbalpha}_{r}:{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

as the composition
{\bbalpha}^{\dagger}_{r}({\bbiota}^{\dagger}_{\operatorname{syn}})^{-1}
. From what we have shown above, it follows that:

Corollary 7.37
.

The natural map of VS’s (with values in
{\mathscr{D}}(C_{{\bf Q}_{p}})
)

\tau_{\leq r}{\bbalpha}_{r}:\tau_{\leq r}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{syn}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))\to\tau_{\leq r}{\mathbb{R}}_{\operatorname{pro\acute{e}t}}(X,{\bf Q}_{p}(r))

is a strict quasi-isomorphism.
